* ikiro

** intent

today: three JDexes on disk, unindexed; the company's mail and chat live in other people's
apps; OpenExecutive as reference; =@education= as the template for how a domain, its modes
and its data are built.

after: a tapped =@vcompany= package whose =vivalence= instance boots one office daemon that
owns documents (=vdex=) and messages (=email= · =chat=) as literals, syncs them from the
world on boot, serves them to apps and to agentic modes through tools, and carries the chief
officers — the company operators — as consumers of those tools. The ledger (=transaction=) is
a fourth, empty slot.

why office-first (beef): /"All of this is reference material. I want you to now start
thinking basically from scratch."/ — the officers need documents and an inbox before they
need a persona; the port of OpenExecutive is the last stage, not the first.

** decisions (mine — every one overturnable)

- the c·o family lands as mode type =officer=; slugs are the acronyms (beef's rows): =officer/ceo= "Chief Executive Officer" · "company operator" is the letterless chief — the orchestrator OpenExecutive calls the executive; =officer/cfo= "Chief Finance Officer" · "company operator — finance"; =officer/cto= "Chief Technology Officer" · "company operator — technology"; the rest of the roster at the officer stage (=clo= legal · =cso= strategy · =cao= administration · =cpo= product · =cmo= marketing · =cio= information · =cgo= grants). =manifest.name= = the name, =manifest.description= = the description. The office modes (=vdex= · =email= · =chat=) stay type =office= (unruled); =chief= · =executive= · =specialist= are gone. T0 ships the three officers as skeletons (manifest + one harness line) so the package has its whole shape from the first boot.
- the traits are beef's nouns: =DOCUMENT= · =MESSAGE= · =MAIL= · =CHAT= · =TRANSACTION= — not participles.
- =message= is ONE ontology; channel = symbol dimension, channel-shaped fields = traits (MAIL · CHAT). Two ontologies would fork every read tool and the subscriber allows one TOPOGRAPHICAL symbol anyway.
- vdex slug = deepest JD coordinate · folded stem · extension: =22.04.re24-05277.pdf= · =12.04.ubiquity-and-a-thin-slice.md= · =12.notes.md= (category-level file) · =readme.org= (no coordinate). Directories never enter the slug; =DOCUMENT.asset.path= is the truth on disk. Collision (same stem+ext under one coordinate in two subfolders) → sorted by path, second gets =-2= before the extension.
- the JD id is a SYMBOL (=vdex.id.22.04=), a file is a LITERAL; nothing else is a row. The standard-slot symbols are cut to =vdex.slot.index= (NN.00); inbox · tasks · templates · links · someday · archive are skipped for now (beef).
- formats: =statics.formats = ["md","org","json","txt","pdf"]= from day one. =DOCUMENT.readable= = format ∈ formats = the App renders it — text through Textarea + Markdown/Org/Json, pdf through drapes =Pdf= (T1.b, "requires propper drapes"). Tools read TEXT only (=types.js= =TEXT=); =vdex_fs_read= on a pdf answers the cargo url and "binary — open it in the app" until an =ocr= service exists.
- the per-mode config = the object kernel entry ={module, mountpoint, statics, secrets}=. MASKING IS PALADIN'S (beef): the pinhole hydrates the entry (LANDED), =dress= gives it its =mountpoint= — declared ABSOLUTE, nothing else (his /"no automatic scopes"/: nothing resolved against the instance file, nothing minted; a relative string THROWS at mount naming the slot, a blank stays REQUIRED) — =integrate= creates a declared dir at init like the daemon's, and =vip.accioOne= lays the instance statics over the module's when it forms the ={service, mask}= pair. The runtime READS three fields onto the citizen. Nothing is minted or merged runtime-side; the =outfit()= of the first draft is gone. Field named =mountpoint= (house noun for a data dir), not "mountpath".
- a string kernel entry has no mask: the module's statics, no secrets, NO mountpoint. A mode that needs a data dir is declared in object form — that is what the object form is for.
- the noun law: =mount= = where a thing is attached (a route in typology and the runtime — =App= · =View= · =daemon= · =mode= · =AIMED=; a disk location in the ledger — =instances.json=, =*_MOUNT=); =mountpoint= = a data dir (=scope.mountpoint= → =daemon.mountpoint= → =mode.mountpoint=). Neither renames. Paladin's own wobble — =mask.mount= for the daemon/service data dir, renamed by the runtime's =wiring()= — STAYS: his /"keep two mount"/; the "one noun" hunk set is RETIRED (kept below for the record).
- the JDex lives WHEREVER HE KEEPS IT (his /"jdex path will be somehwere in my system. no automatic scopes."/): the vdex =mountpoint= is an ABSOLUTE path in =daemon.js= — today =/Users/finn/vivalence/coorporation= on the template AND the instance — no =VIVA_VDEX_MOUNT=, no =./jdex=, nothing follows the instance, nothing is minted. The package is code + instance recipe; =.env= and =mountpoint/= inside it are covered by the package's OWN =.gitignore= (=**/.env= · =**/mountpoint= — the vivalence root's does not reach the store), written with the README. The instance is slugged =vcompany= in the registry (the template); the LIVE one is beef's instance copy =~/.viva/instances/vivalence= (his =daemon.js= there carries only =voffice= + =vdex= in the kernel — the rest commented out, his edit, canonical).
- an unset =mountpoint= never silently defaults: the thunk is recorded by =hydrate=, =instance/doctor= reports =… REQUIRED at daemon[vcompany].kernel[1].mountpoint=, =dress= leaves the blank alone, and the m52 gate (=check.instance().throw()=) refuses boot before =modes()= runs. An entry that declares none has none (=email= · =chat= today).
- messages are KERNEL rows (daemon-holistic, like literals), not userspace Turns: the company inbox belongs to the daemon, not to a user. An officer that answers projects the message into its own thread.
- reply threading rides the literal's EXISTING self m:n (=uses= / =in=, =kernel/Literal.ts=): a reply =uses= its parent. No thread entity for messages.
- message bodies live IN the row (small); document bodies never do (freight serves them).
- attachments = mode freight entries referenced from =MAIL.attachments[]=; filing one into the vdex is a tool call (=vdex_fs_write=), not automatic.
- boot-time datasets reuse the DATASET install (=install(mode, daemon, dataset)= factored out of the trait); no =installed= stamp for boot installs — they always run. Boot rows are =new Dataset({symbol: rows, literal: rows})= — =reader.lift= already takes arrays.
- prune in v1 is BY ONTOLOGY, not by owner: the vdex mode is the only writer of =vdex= rows and of =vdex.{area,category,id}.*= symbols in this package, so =literal.prune("vdex", keep)= and =symbol.prune(["vdex.area","vdex.category","vdex.id"], keep)= on the voffice repositories delete exactly what boot no longer sees; scheme rows never match a prefix. T1.a (mode↔literal/symbol on typology level — beef's wish) stays wanted and GATED but no longer blocks vdex; when it lands, the boot switches one call to the owner form.
- no watcher in v1: boot indexes, every own write reindexes its file (one =describe=, one upsert, one =freight.admit=); external edits land at the next boot.
- editor = drapes =Textarea=; renderers = drapes =Markdown= (lifted from kajuit) · =Org= (new) · =Json= (exists) · =Pdf= (new — an =<object>= over the cargo url).
- the m27 gate (memory/notes) moves to the officer stage; T0–T3 do not wait on it.
- outbound sends in T2 are tool calls the operator sees, not decision-gated; the gate is the officer stage's.
- a mode's files never import from outside the mode's own directory (beef: /"no leaving the mode!"/ — the callout; the standing rule from aprende: /"thats not how a registry works"/). The vdex owns =types.js= (=TEXT=) and its own line formatter; a domain export is reached at runtime through =daemon.domain.*= or not at all. The domain's =types.js= drops =TEXT= · =FORMATS= · =COORDINATE= — nothing in the domain read them.
- the ignore doctrine (his /"think holistically"/, then the connoisseur's pass on his /"something is off"/): ONE declaration =subsystems/paladin/belt/ignore.js= — =IGNORE=, a glob list with =*.bak= in it as data, and =skip(globs = IGNORE)= compiling it once into a name predicate; the walker's head takes the list beside the pattern (=find.walk(pattern, ignore)(root, depth)= · =find.index(root, ignore)=), the call shape unchanged; a caller's list is the WHOLE list (no union, no "extras") — FRAUGHT passes =mode.statics?.ignore=, the fs skill reads =ctx.ignore= bound beside =ctx.root=, the instance's =statics.ignore= carries the house names + =jdex-sbs=; =describe= is a stat. Not a gitignore-style file in the tree: index-ignore ≠ vcs-ignore.
- the vdex is a dataset SOURCE (his WEIRD on the =install()= export): typology =reader.load= (one holy line, T1's second beside the MIME table), =load.js= = the two loaders over one scan + a stat-only fingerprint, DATASET installs, BOOTED drains + prunes, =reindex= = blank the stamp + run the trait. No trait body is exported or split.
- the symbol fold is MEASURED (=gestalten/belt/object.js place()=): the last segment becomes a string value — =symbol.vdex = { area: "20-29", category: "22", id: { "22": "04" }, format: "pdf" }=; =vdex= alone folds to =true=. The T1 text that showed nested empty objects was wrong; =index.js= walks the measured shape.
- the tool envelope is the m26 yield contract everywhere in this package: ={condition?, output: {message?, object?, literal?: [], buffer?: []}}= — entity keys SINGULAR. A =.use= middleware short-circuits by SETTING =ctx.output= and not calling =next= (=strategy.js resolve()= reads the effect's return only). The T0 =lookup.js= is re-enveloped in T1 files.
- the JD index slot labels its coordinate: a =NN.00= file's title becomes the LABELED name of the category it sits in (or the area, when directly under one) — the docs site's own =jdex.js indexOf()= rule, and Johnny.Decimal's (the =00.00= file IS the index). The tree still mints the coordinates; the index files hand them their names.
- the package's test fixtures live at the PACKAGE root (=~/.viva/registry/vcompany/tests/scenarios.js=: =office(viva, {mountpoint})= mounts the voffice repositories under a mode, =jdex()= builds a scratch tree) — a mode's tests reach the package's fixtures, never another module's files.

** ontologies — the law and the three

House mechanism, unchanged (=@education=): an ontology IS the one TOPOGRAPHICAL symbol on a
literal (=Literal.ontology=; =kernel/Literal.ts= subscriber throws on two); traits carry
payload shape (=traits[]= + =trait{}=); symbols classify (ONTOLOGICAL = subject matter,
STRUCTURAL = sets, LABELED = name + description). A domain extends the runtime literal with
its trait enum and repository verbs.

#+begin_example
literal
├── vdex         a file on the mount        DOCUMENT  {asset:{path}, title, format, readable, bytes, mtime, hash}   body NOT in row
├── message      a message on a channel     MESSAGE   {channel, direction, from, to[], at, body, external:{id}}     body IN row
│                                           MAIL    {subject, cc[], messageId, inReplyTo, references[], attachments[]}
│                                           CHAT   {platform, chat, sender:{id,name}, replyTo}
└── transaction  (T3, empty)                TRANSACTION    {at, memo, entries:[{account, amount, currency}]}  Σ amount = 0
#+end_example

Documents are VOCALIZED's shape over a text mount: =VOCALIZED {asset:{path}}= is served by
the FRAUGHT machinery (=systems/runtime/daemon/traits/index.js:43= — a =Freight= over a
root, url =/cargo/…=); =DOCUMENT {asset:{path}}= rides the same with root = the mode's
mountpoint and =md org txt= in the MIME table.

** research — openexecutive · jdex (on order, this session)

*** OpenExecutive

- checkout =/Users/finn/code/OpenExecutive= at =6e15d65= (the pin =36ca0e1= + provider/auth docs + a utf-8 file-io fix); upstream =main= is two commits further (=ba30769=: an OpenRouter council model dropdown) — nothing that touches the port. 3.9k stars · 387 forks · Apache-2.0 · Sonnet 5 default, Opus 5 for deep reasoning · FastAPI + Next.js 15 · ChromaDB + SQLite.
- the package =packages/core/openexecutive/= has 27 directories; =orchestrator/executive.py= is 1559 lines beside 11k lines of tool modules (calendar · schedule · talent · people · workflows · mcp gateway); =agents/= is eight 16–17-line files (a prompt import + a spec) plus =overrides.py= 365 · =triage.py= 322 · =base.py= 216; =prompts/domain_prompts.py= (252 lines) holds the nine role prompts — =CSO_PROMPT= :1 · =CFO_PROMPT= :29 · =CHRO_PROMPT= :57 · =GC_PROMPT= :86 · =COO_PROMPT= :113 · =CMO_PROMPT= :141 · =CPO_PROMPT= :169 · =BOARD_COMMS_PROMPT= :197 · =TALENT_PROMPT= :225 (energy-sector search, skip); =prompts/executive_persona.py= (166) is the orchestrator's voice; =memory/decision_ledger.py= 458 · =episodic.py= 1901 · =honcho_client.py= 1438 (skip).
- =knowledge/builtin/= is MBA prose by department — =board/= 6 · =finance/= 9 (accounting basics · capital allocation · financial modeling · financial statements · fundraising · ipo readiness · saas metrics · unit economics · valuation) · =hr/= 8 · =failures/= 15 case studies (enron · svb · theranos · wework …).
- for the officer stage: =cfo= ← =CFO_PROMPT= + =knowledge/builtin/finance/*= (attributed); =ceo= ← =executive_persona.py=; =cto= has NO source there (their =CPO= is product, not technology) — authored new; their roster has no CTO and no CIO.

*** JDex — the doctrine, from the source

- johnnydecimal.com =11.05 The JDex=: /"The index is your Johnny.Decimal system"/ · /"An index is not an inventory. It is for recording the IDs that you have assigned, not to track all of your documents."/ · one entry per ID = id + title + location(s) (/"Email, chat, cloud storage, hard drives, servers, apps…"/) + keywords · /"Creating the note is creating the new ID."/ · /"Your filesystem is not your index."/
- blog 0182 (data and storage): an entry is metadata ABOVE the line (standardised keys — =Location= · =Data= · =Last updated=) and free data BELOW; the recommended storage is /partially nested/ — the JDex files at =00.00=, never scattered through the tree.
- the Small Business System (=jdex-sbs=, 692 files — the €350 template from the 2025.11.19 log): every category carries the standard zeros =.00 JDex= · =.01 Inbox= · =.02 Task & project management= · =.03 Templates= · =.04 Links= · =.08 Someday= · =.09 Archive=; beef cut ours to =.00=.
- JDHQ ships a CLI, an API and an MCP server (beta) — a later interop, not v1.

*** the three JDexes, measured

- =~/vivalence/coorporation= (git): =10-19_vivalence= empty · =20-29_koerperschaft= = 20 formation (corporation · notary · registry) / 21 compliance / 22 finance (banking · accounting) / 23 agreements (nda) — 24 pdf, one org, one tex · =30-39_funding= = 30 applications (content md ×4 · openinternetstack · innorampup) · =jdex-sbs/= the template (351 md + 347 txt are mostly it) · no =00.00= · a stray =README.md~=.
- =~/vivalence/organization= (jj): =00.00 Index.org= is five stale lines; =10-19_viva/{11_identity, 12_goals (11 files), 13_organization, 18_vinca}= — org + md; =20-29_repository= empty; =readme.org= says /"jdex based scaffold idea for an open source organizations identity"/.
- =documentation/content= (the docs site): =NN-NN_area/NN_category/NN.NN_page.mdx=; =content/jdex.js= derives =AREAS= / =CATEGORIES= from disk and a =NN.00_*.mdx= stub's frontmatter title names its area or category — the rule the vdex boot now shares.
- beef's log doctrine (2025.10.06 · 2025.10.31 · 2025.11.19 · 2026.05.14): jdex for filesystem and conceptual structure, c4 · divio · pacelayers beside it; /"need a idea for a registry jdex ontology"/ — still open.

*** what the research changed in T1

- the =NN.00= title labels its coordinate (docs rule = JD rule) · the symbol fold shape corrected from measurement · the ignore doctrine answered holistically · a T2 hook: a JDex entry's /location/ may be an inbox — message literals may carry =vdex.id.*= symbols so one coordinate groups documents AND mail (fork, T2).

** the mechanisms

*** ownership — mode ↔ literal · symbol (typology + runtime, GATED, T1.a)

Today a dataset row has no owner: =Mode.installed= is a content hash, a literal knows its
symbols and its =uses=, nothing knows which mode put it there. Prune is therefore impossible
everywhere. Typology =schematics/entities/{mode,literal,symbol}.js= gain the rel arrays
(mirror of =buffer.js:12-13=); runtime =Mode.ts= gains the owner m:n (pivots
=mode_literals= · =mode_symbols=); =traits/dataset.js= adds the owner in its link phase —
which now runs THROUGH THE ORM (sibling session, =known-issues.org=
=literal-ontology-blank-after-dataset-install= RESOLVED): beside =from[prop].add(to)= it does
=from.modes.add(owner)=, one =flush()= per chunk, the subscriber fires once with everything
initialized. =prune(mode, keep)= lands on the runtime =LiteralRepository= / =SymbolRepository=
(generic: every dataset mode can use it). Education's rows gain owners; its boot is otherwise
byte-identical — and =stamp()= folds the installer's own source, so the =dataset.js= change
reinstalls every DATASET mode at the next boot and backfills the owners.

v1 does not wait for it: the vdex boot prunes by ontology and symbol prefix on the voffice
repositories (decisions). T1.a adds the owner rows and the general =prune(mode, keep)=; the
boot then switches one call.

*** per-mode config — the object kernel entry (T0)

#+begin_example
declaration   daemon.js   kernel: [ "@vcompany/domain/voffice", { module, mountpoint: "./jdex", statics }, { module: "@vcompany/office/email", statics }, "@vcompany/officer/ceo" ]
pinhole       paladin     materialize() → reference() → hydrate(entry, record, "daemon[vcompany].kernel[1]")    thunks fire, unset keys recorded              LANDED
dress         paladin     mountpoint: declared absolute · declared relative to the instance file · minted <daemon mountpoint>/mode_<type>_<slug>          READY
settle        paladin     Instance.cast (union already admits the object) · doctor "… REQUIRED at daemon[vcompany].kernel[1].mountpoint" for a blank thunk existing
integrate     paladin     statements() creates every mountpoint at init — daemons, services, and now kernel masks                                          READY
accio         paladin     vip.accioOne → { service: <module>, mask: { …entry, statics: {…module.statics, …entry.statics} } }                               READY
read          runtime     modes(): new Mode(service) · statics = mask.statics ?? module's · secrets = mask.secrets ?? {} · mountpoint = mask.mountpoint ?? null   READY
metadata      runtime     /mode/office/vdex/metadata/{statics,mountpoint}                                          never secrets                            READY
#+end_example

String entries have no mask: the module's statics, no secrets, no mountpoint.

*** boot-time datasets (T1)

The tree IS a dataset source. =reader.load= (typology — the second holy line, beside b3's MIME
table) lets a mode declare ={load(mode) → rows, stamp(mode) → string}= beside =rows= · =read= ·
=walk=; DATASET pulls it through the same chunked upsert + ORM link phase, and =stamp()= folds the
loader's stamp so =resolution.js:49-56= reruns the install only when the fingerprint moved.
=BOOTED= (=traits/booted.js=) runs =boot(daemon, mode)= after every trait is wired: it drains the
scan the two loaders shared and prunes by ontology + prefix. His /"WEIRD"/ on the first cut — the
trait's body split into an exported =install()= for the mode to call — is what this replaces. The
email/chat sync lands its rows through the voffice repository one message at a time (=external.id=
idempotent), no prune.

*** cargo over the mount (T1)

FRAUGHT's root becomes =mode.mountpoint?.absolute ?? mode.module.mount.dirname + freight.path=
(=traits/index.js:44=, T1.c). A declared mountpoint that does not exist yet is created at init
by paladin's =integrate=, like the daemon's — a fresh instance boots with an empty JDex.
=vdex= declares =new Freight("/")= — the mountpoint IS the cargo; bodies are fetched by path
(freight url or =fs_read=), never carried in the row.

*** boot as a dataset — beef's alternative (FORK, his call)

Today, measured: =Dataset= sources are THREE readers — =rows= · =read + codec= · =walk=
(=subsystems/typology/prototypes/dataset.js:5-17=); the runtime =pull()= resolves them
(=traits/dataset.js:16-20=) and =stamp()= hashes the SOURCE FILES to gate a reinstall
(=lifecycle/resolution.js:49-56=: stamp differs → =installed = ""= → DATASET runs). No loader,
no prune, no owner. =Datasink= writes ROWS as canonical json/js/jsonl into the MODULE dir
(=traits/datasink.js:50=, =Datasink.canonical=), twitch-driven off =/after/<type>/<op>= — the
literal-level twitches he names ALREADY exist (=shape.subscriber= → =daemon.twitch=), debounced 1.5 s.

His shape:

#+begin_src javascript
export const dataset = new Dataset({
  symbol: "dataset/symbols.js",
  literal: { load: index, stamp: fingerprint },          // the tree IS the source — ① a fourth reader
});
export const datasink = new Datasink({
  literal: [[{ ontology: "vdex", "trait.DOCUMENT.readable": true }, project.body, writer.text("%", { root: "mountpoint" })]],   // ③ one file per row
});
#+end_src

What it takes — three gated touches:
- ① =reader.load= — a fourth source ={load(mode) → rows, stamp(mode) → string}=; runtime =pull()= calls it, =stamp()= folds its stamp (a stat-only =find.index= — paths + bytes + mtime, nothing read) → DATASET reruns on every boot the tree changed, the same chunked upsert; =installed= stays the gate. LANDED in =* T1= on his WEIRD — the vdex declares its loaders in either shape.
- ② owner rows by default (T1.a) + DATASET prunes =owner ∧ ∉ fresh= after install → prune is generic, BOOTED leaves the vdex. /"we'd still need to prune on boot"/ — yes, this is it. /"remove mode ownership on sink"/ — sinks already select by =where=, never by owner; nothing to remove.
- ③ the reverse direction needs a per-row TEXT writer rooted at the mountpoint (today: rows → one canonical file in the module dir) AND the body on the row — =DOCUMENT.body= for TEXT formats under a cap, pdf never — today bodies are fetched by path, never carried. With it, every edit path converges on the entity (=entity_update= · kajuit · the App) and the sink writes the file; the App's =/write= stops touching disk.
- live disk changes: DATASET runs at boot; own writes reindex; FOREIGN writes need =Deno.watchFs= (no precedent in the repo) → debounce → =reindex(path)= — its teardown is exactly BOOTED's =terminate= slot, so a watcher is T1.d in EITHER design.

Verdict: possible. ① is T1's own now (WEIRD); what remains for =* T1.#2= is ② (T1.a onto the
critical path) and ③ (a =Datasink= writer + bodies in rows). Gain: prune generic, one write path,
the JDex is literally a dataset both ways. Cost: two holy touches after the first =[VDEX]= line.
Lean: T1 first (one holy line beside the MIME table — rollout steps 3–7), ② ③ at step 8 —
=load.js= IS the loader either way, =reindex= IS the watcher's callback either way.

*** T0 — package setup
deps: none. No typology touch (the "one noun" set touches =Mask= — 2 lines, separately gated).
enables: a live daemon with the domain, every scheme symbol and three officer skeletons installed; harness answers
as bot; T1/T2 land into a running instance; =mode.statics= / =mode.secrets= / =mode.mountpoint= reach a mode from
the instance declaration, dressed by paladin.
done: =viva instance/doctor vivalence --json= ok · =registry/list= shows =@vcompany= · kajuit lists the daemon with
=office/{vdex,email,chat}= + =officer/{ceo,cfo,cto}= · =entities/symbol/find {slug:{$like:"vdex.%"}}= returns the
scheme · =GET …/mode/office/email/metadata/statics= answers ={address: "office@vivalence.org", poll: 60}= ·
=GET …/mode/office/vdex/metadata/mountpoint= answers =…/instances/vcompany/jdex= · =ls …/mountpoint/daemon_vcompany/=
shows =mode_office_email mode_office_chat= after init · paladin + runtime suites at baseline plus the new cases ·
italian + hello-world boot byte-identical.

*** T1 — vdex
T1.a ownership — deps T0. GATED (typology holy; ask, then blast-bracket) and DECOUPLED: vdex v1 prunes by ontology; T1.a lands whenever and generalizes it.
  done: italian boot byte-identical in rows and log lines + =mode_literals= populated · typology · runtime suites at baseline · =prune(mode, [])= on a scratch mode deletes exactly its rows.
T1.b paladin fs + drapes — deps none (parallel to T1.a).
  done: =subsystems/paladin/tests/skills.test.js= extended · =find.index= sorted, a caller's list is the whole list · =fs_move= refuses to escape the root · drapes =Markdown= renders what kajuit's did, byte-for-byte on the fixture · =Pdf= shows a pdf from a cargo url.
T1.c the vdex mode — deps T0 · T1.b (T1.a optional — it swaps one prune call).
  enables: every agentic mode reads and writes the company's documents through tools; the officers' "knowledge" IS the JDex; the operator edits in kajuit.
  done: boot logs =[VDEX] <mount> N documents · M coordinates · pruned x/y= · edit a file in kajuit → disk changes → reboot keeps it · delete a file on disk → reboot prunes its row and any orphaned coordinate · chat "what is filed under 22" answers from the harness section without a tool call · "read the DKB Antrag" → =vdex_find= → =vdex_fs_read= → text · a pdf opens in the App through =Pdf=; =vdex_fs_read= on it answers "binary — open it in the app" with the cargo url.

*** T2 — messaging
deps: T1.a (ownership) · T1.c (the boot/sync shape) · a Deno mail dep (fork).
enables: the company's conversations are literals; the officers read and reply through the same tools the operator has.
done: a mail arrives → literal with MAIL, attachment in freight · =email_send= lands in the real mailbox and appears as =message.direction.out= · telegram DM round-trips · =voffice_lookup {text}= finds across both channels · =uses= links a reply to its parent.

*** T3 — bookkeeping
empty. Ontology =transaction=, trait =TRANSACTION=, own daemon + db, Σ entries = 0 in the repository. A milestone slot.

*** the officer stage (after T0–T3; beef's earlier M53.2–M53.9 folded)
deps: T0–T2 · m27 M27.1 + M27.2 (memory/notes projections; the ADVERTISED upgrade) · a decision ontology.
- scaffold: =officer/ceo= — name "Chief Executive Officer", description "company operator" (the T0 skeleton, filled) — persona from =executive_persona.py= near-verbatim (the "holding the line" + autonomy-tiers prose is strong); air-gap fallback answers as bot
- the roster: =officer/{cfo,cto}= (T0 skeletons, filled) + =officer/{clo,cso,cao,cgo,cpo,cmo,cio}= — names "Chief Finance Officer" · "Chief Technology Officer" · "Chief Legal Officer" …, descriptions "company operator — finance" …; prompts from =prompts/domain_prompts.py= + =agents/*.py= where OpenExecutive has one (finance · legal · strategy · product · marketing), new where vivalence's reality differs (cao = the Körperschaft paperwork · cgo = 30-39_funding · cio = the vdex steward · cto = the repo); one =/consult= tool each; AGENTIC (=traits/agentic.js=) arms every TOOLED mode under =/<slug>= — =cfo_consult= · =vdex_find= · =email_send= — zero dispatch code, a tenth officer is a kernel line
- knowledge: DONE by T1 — the vdex index in the harness, tools for depth; no cards, no chunker, no embeddings
- decisions: =decision= ontology (fourth literal ontology, or a userspace entity — fork), their state machine VERBATIM with compare-and-set transitions, post-turn extraction (cheap faculty), =system.decisions= projection. Source fact: OpenExecutive keeps TWO tables — episodic =decisions= (haiku extraction) and =decision_instances= (the gated state machine); extraction never touches the state machine. =outbound_guard.py= is anti-spam (dedup · rate · quiet hours, FAIL-OPEN), not the gate — the gate is =departments/authority.py= + the ledger's =gate_mode=.
- scheduler + workflows: claim-based runner over libsql; morning-brief · eod-digest · investor-update · board-prep as Vector pipelines
- channels: DONE by T2 · ledger: T3 · evals: =packages/core/openexecutive/evals/_scenarios/*.yaml= + =fixtures/companies/*/scenarios/= (NOT =evals/scenarios/= — README stale) → fixture package, judge via harness

#+begin_src javascript
// modes/officer/ceo/ceo.viva.js
export const manifest = {
  type: "officer", slug: "ceo", name: "Chief Executive Officer", description: "company operator",
  version: "0.1.0", traits: ["CONVERSATIONAL", "HARNESSED", "TOOLED", "AGENTIC", "APPLICATION", "STANDALONE", "EXPOSED"],
};
// modes/officer/ceo/harness.js — keyed sections, stable first (the cache mark is key order); replaces the T0 one-liner
export const harness = new Vector().use(async (ctx, next) => {
  ctx.hallucination.policy.tune ??= "capable";
  ctx.hallucination.system.persona = persona;                                        // constant
  ctx.hallucination.system.company = profile(ctx.daemon.statics.company);           // the daemon statics — the instance IS the company profile
  ctx.hallucination.system.vdex = await ctx.daemon.modes.office.vdex.call.index();    // the same index the vdex harness shows itself
  ctx.hallucination.system.inbox = await ctx.daemon.modes.office.email.call.inbox({ limit: 12 });
  await next();
});
// modes/officer/cfo/cfo.viva.js
export const manifest = {
  type: "officer", slug: "cfo", name: "Chief Finance Officer", description: "company operator — finance: 22_finance, funding, the ledger",
  version: "0.1.0", traits: ["HARNESSED", "TOOLED"],                                 // not AGENTIC: an officer never consults
};
// modes/officer/cfo/tools/consult.js — the one tool; the chief sees it as cfo_consult
export const consult = new Vector().open(
  { nature: "/consult", valence: "Consult the Chief Finance Officer — …", input: v.object({ query: v.string(), context: v.string().optional() }) },
  async (ctx) => {
    const { output } = await ctx.mode.harness.object.render({
      turns: [{ role: "user", parts: [{ type: "text", text: [ctx.input.context && `<context>\n${ctx.input.context}\n</context>`, ctx.input.query].filter(Boolean).join("\n\n") }] }],
      output: v.object({ analysis: v.string() }),
    });
    return { message: output.object.analysis };                                       // single voice: the chief synthesizes, never relays
  },
);
#+end_src

└── instances/vcompany/                    @vcompany/instance/vcompany — the TEMPLATE; the live instance = beef's instance copy ~/.viva/instances/vivalence
    ├── instance.viva.js · daemon.js
    ├── jdex/                              the JDex, whole — the vdex mountpoint (committed with the package)
    ├── .env                               ignored by the package's own ignore file (**/.env)
    └── mountpoint/                        ignored (**/mountpoint) — daemon_vcompany/{vcompany.viva.db, mode_office_email/, …}
#+end_example

*** domain — =@vcompany/domain/voffice=

The trait enum the domain literal carries — the whole ontology set declared once. Names are
beef's (T0 comment: /"@BeEF! trait TRANSACTION"/; =MAIL= · =CHAT= in the enum he edited):

#+begin_src typescript
export enum LiteralTraitsEnum {
  DOCUMENT = "DOCUMENT",         // vdex
  MESSAGE = "MESSAGE",           // message
  MAIL = "MAIL",                 // message.channel.email
  CHAT = "CHAT",                 // message.channel.{telegram,slack}
  TRANSACTION = "TRANSACTION",   // transaction (T3)
}
#+end_src

*** modes — the rows the Mode table holds after T0

#+begin_example
type       slug     name                       description                                               traits                        installed
domain     voffice  The Office                 Documents, messages and (later) transactions as literals  EXPOSED,TOOLED                ""
office     vdex     VDex                       The JDex on disk as literals …                            DATASET,EXPOSED,HARNESSED     <hash>
office     email    Mail                       The office mailbox as literals …                          DATASET,EXPOSED,HARNESSED     <hash>
office     chat     Chat                       The office chats as literals …                            DATASET,EXPOSED,HARNESSED     <hash>
officer    ceo      Chief Executive Officer    company operator                                          HARNESSED,EXPOSED             ""
officer    cfo      Chief Finance Officer      company operator — finance                                HARNESSED,EXPOSED             ""
officer    cto      Chief Technology Officer   company operator — technology                             HARNESSED,EXPOSED             ""
#+end_example

The officers are skeletons: manifest + one harness line (persona name + the company profile
from the daemon statics), answering as the bot without a key. The officer stage fills them
(persona port · tools · AGENTIC). Slugs are the acronyms — beef's rows; =officer/ceo= is gone,
=ceo= is the letterless chief. T1 adds =BOOTED · FRAUGHT · APPLICATION · STANDALONE · TOOLED=
to vdex; T2 the same to email and chat. Traits ACCUMULATE on the row (=population.js:134=
=array.unique=), so the T0 rows never need touching.

message.direction.{in,out}            ONTOLOGICAL LABELED
message.status.{unread,read,answered} STRUCTURAL LABELED
message.channel.email                 ONTOLOGICAL LABELED                 email declares it
message.channel.{telegram,slack}      ONTOLOGICAL LABELED                 chat declares them
#+end_example

*** the resolved Mode after the mask — what the harness and =/metadata= see

Masking is paladin's: the kernel entry is hydrated, its =mountpoint= declared (absolute) or absent, its
statics laid over the module's — all at the pinhole and the accio. The runtime READS.

#+begin_example
mode.type        "office"
mode.slug        "vdex"
mode.statics     { formats: ["md","org","json","txt","pdf"], ignore: ["bak","archive","slp","node_modules",".git",".DS_Store","jdex-sbs"] }   module ← entry, key by key
mode.secrets     {}
mode.mountpoint  Path("/Users/finn/vivalence/coorporation")                              declared absolute — nothing resolved, nothing minted
mode.mount       Path("/mode/office/vdex")                                                the ROUTING mount, unchanged — see the noun law below
mode.module      the pensieve module (shared, never mutated)

mode.slug        "email"
mode.statics     { address: "office@vivalence.org", poll: 60 }
mode.mountpoint  null                                                                     declares none — none (his "no automatic scopes")

mode.slug        "voffice"  (a STRING kernel entry: no mask)
mode.statics     the module's · mode.secrets {} · mode.mountpoint null
#+end_example

The noun law (beef on =mode.mount=: /"other name?? or move mountpoint ??"/): =mount= is WHERE A
THING IS ATTACHED — a URL path in typology and the runtime (=App.mount= · =View.mount= ·
=daemon.mount= =/daemon/<slug>= · =mode.mount= · =AIMED.mount=, ~40 readers), a filesystem
path in the ledger (=instances.json= =mount=, =*_MOUNT= env vars — "MOUNT MEANS PATH"). The
DATA DIR is a =mountpoint=: =scope.mountpoint= → =daemon.mountpoint= → =mode.mountpoint=.
The daemon already carries both (=good.mount= · =good.mountpoint=); the mode mirrors it.
Neither is renamed. The one wobble is paladin's own: =mask()= names the daemon data dir
=mount= and the runtime's =wiring()= renames it (=good.mountpoint = mask.mount=), and the
=Mask= prototype hands that =mount= to the datamap (libsql reads =datamap.mount= for the db
file). One noun, pinhole to runtime — the "one noun" hunk set below (touches the typology
=Mask= prototype, two lines: your go).

*** environment — the doctor's view after =instances/use vivalence init=

No =VIVA_VDEX_MOUNT=: the JDex path is declared absolute in =daemon.js= (beef: /"jdex path will be
somehwere in my system. no automatic scopes."/), never in the env. The pinhole still records a
thunk in a kernel entry (=settle.test.js= keeps the law) — vcompany just does not use one.

#+begin_example
SECRET_VIVA_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY  keys      optional                                              blank = every office mode and officer answers as the bot
VIVA_MAIL_IMAP · VIVA_MAIL_SMTP · SECRET_VIVA_MAIL_* · SECRET_VIVA_TELEGRAM_TOKEN   optional   T2 flips the mail secrets to required when the daemon consumes the service
#+end_example

*** subsystems/paladin/tests/settle.test.js
LANDED — the =describe= landed with (a) grows with (b): five cases on the file's own =mount()= fixture
(=scope.mountpoint= = =/mountpoint=, =HOME= = =/fixtures/probe/test.viva.js=). The relative case has
its own fixture because it throws at mount.

#+begin_src javascript
const office = (paladin) =>
  daemon({
    kernel: [
      "@vcompany/domain/voffice",
      { module: "@vcompany/office/vdex", mountpoint: () => paladin.env.get("VIVA_PROBE_VDEX"), statics: { formats: ["md"] } },
      { module: "@vcompany/office/email", statics: { poll: 60 } },
    ],
  });

const relativeInstance = (paladin) => ({
  ...officeInstance(paladin),
  daemons: [daemon({ kernel: [{ module: "@vcompany/office/chat", mountpoint: "./chats" }] })],
});

describe("settle — a kernel entry in object form fires at the pinhole like any mask", () => {
  it("the mountpoint thunk fires and lands as a Path; the entry keeps its module and statics; the string entry beside it is untouched", async () => {
    const instance = await mount(officeInstance, { ...SET, VIVA_PROBE_VDEX: "/jdex" });
    const [domain, entry] = instance.daemons[0].kernel;
    expect(domain).toBe("@vcompany/domain/voffice");
    expect(entry.module).toBe("@vcompany/office/vdex");
    expect(entry.mountpoint).toBeInstanceOf(Path);
    expect(entry.mountpoint.absolute).toBe("/jdex");
    expect(entry.statics).toEqual({ formats: ["md"] });
    expect(instance.faults).toEqual([]);
  });

  it("an unset mountpoint is REQUIRED at its slot — the doctor names the entry; nothing is minted over it", async () => {
    const instance = await mount(officeInstance, SET);
    expect(sentences(instance)).toContain("VIVA_PROBE_VDEX REQUIRED at daemon[probe].kernel[1].mountpoint");
    expect(instance.daemons[0].kernel[1].mountpoint).toBeNull();
  });

  it("an entry without a mountpoint stays without one — no data dir is minted", async () => {
    const instance = await mount(officeInstance, { ...SET, VIVA_PROBE_VDEX: "/jdex" });
    expect(instance.daemons[0].kernel[2].mountpoint).toBeUndefined();
    expect(instance.faults).toEqual([]);
  });

  it("a relative mountpoint is refused at mount — declared means absolute", async () => {
    await expect(mount(relativeInstance, SET)).rejects.toThrow(
      "instance.mount: daemon[probe].kernel[0].mountpoint must be absolute — ./chats",
    );
  });

  it("the daemon's own mount is unchanged by the entries under it", async () => {
    const instance = await mount(officeInstance, { ...SET, VIVA_PROBE_VDEX: "/jdex" });
    expect(instance.daemons[0].mount.absolute).toBe("/mountpoint/daemon_probe");
  });
});
#+end_src

*** ~/.viva/registry/vcompany/README.md

#+begin_src markdown
# @vcompany

The company package for [Vivalence](https://github.com/vivalence/vivalence) — the office (documents · mail · chat) as literals, and the chief officers that operate it.

This is a **package** in the registry store: its modules resolve by `@vcompany/<type>/<slug>` like anything else in the registry, and its `instances/vcompany` is the company's own instance template — the live one sits in `~/.viva/instances` as `vivalence`.

```sh
viva registry/tap ~/.viva/registry/vcompany
viva instances/tap ~/.viva/registry/vcompany/instances/vcompany --slug=vivalence
viva instances/use vivalence init
```

**`domain/voffice`** — `@vcompany/domain/voffice`. One literal, three ontologies: `vdex` (a document on the JDex mount, trait `DOCUMENT`), `message` (a message on a channel, traits `MESSAGE` · `MAIL` · `CHAT`), `transaction` (later, trait `TRANSACTION`). The tools every office mode and every officer shares.

**`modes/office/`** — `vdex` · `email` · `chat`. Each owns its literals, syncs them from the world on boot, and carries its own scheme symbols.

**`modes/officer/`** — the chief officers, company operators: `ceo` · `cfo` · `cto`.

**`instances/vcompany/`** — the company's instance: one office daemon. The JDex lives wherever `daemon.js` points its vdex `mountpoint` — an absolute path, declared, never minted.

## License

Fair Source, as the vivalence repository. Prose ported from [SenteLabsAI/OpenExecutive](https://github.com/SenteLabsAI/OpenExecutive) (Apache-2.0, pinned at `36ca0e1`) is attributed in the file that carries it.
#+end_src

*** ~/.viva/registry/vcompany/domain/voffice/entities/kernel/Literal.ts
new. The =@education= Literal minus retention: the trait enum, =search= over both ontologies,
=card=, and =prune(ontology, keep)= — the vdex mode is the only writer of =vdex= rows in this
package, so an ontology IS an owner here (T1.a's mode↔literal relation generalizes it later).

#+begin_src typescript
import { types, EntitySchema, EntityRepositoryType, type Opt } from "@mikro-orm/core";
import { literal as base } from "@vivalence/runtime";

export enum LiteralTraitsEnum {
  DOCUMENT = "DOCUMENT",
  MESSAGE = "MESSAGE",
  MAIL = "MAIL",
  CHAT = "CHAT",
  TRANSACTION = "TRANSACTION",
}

export class LiteralRepository extends base.repository {
  get card() {
    return {
      fields: ["slug", "ontology", "traits"],
      project: (literal) => ({
        slug: literal.slug,
        ontology: literal.ontology,
        title: literal.trait?.DOCUMENT?.title ?? literal.trait?.MAIL?.subject ?? literal.trait?.MESSAGE?.body?.slice(0, 80) ?? "",
      }),
    };
  }

  search(query: any) {
    const like = `%${query.search}%`;
    return [
      { slug: { $like: like } },
      { trait: { DOCUMENT: { title: { $like: like } } } },
      { trait: { MAIL: { subject: { $like: like } } } },
      { trait: { MESSAGE: { body: { $like: like } } } },
    ];
  }

  prune(ontology: string, keep: string[]) {
    return this.nativeDelete({ ontology, slug: { $nin: keep } });
  }
}

export class LiteralEntity extends base.entity {
  traits: LiteralTraitsEnum[] & Opt = [];
  [EntityRepositoryType]?: LiteralRepository;

  get document() {
    return this.trait.DOCUMENT;
  }

  get message() {
    return this.trait.MESSAGE;
  }

  implements(trait: string): boolean {
    return this.traits.includes(trait as LiteralTraitsEnum);
  }
}

export const LiteralSchema = new EntitySchema({
  class: LiteralEntity,
  extends: base.schema,
  tableName: "Literal",
  name: "Literal",
  repository: () => LiteralRepository,
  properties: {
    traits: {
      items: () => LiteralTraitsEnum,
      enum: true,
      array: true,
      defaultRaw: `'[]'`,
      type: types.json,
    },
  },
});

export default {
  type: "literal",
  traits: LiteralTraitsEnum,
  schema: LiteralSchema,
  entity: LiteralEntity,
  repository: LiteralRepository,
};
#+end_src

*** ~/.viva/registry/vcompany/domain/voffice/entities/kernel/Symbol.ts
new. The base symbol plus =prune(prefixes, keep)= — minted coordinates live under three
prefixes; the scheme rows (=vdex= · =vdex.slot.index= · =vdex.format.*=) never match them.

#+begin_src typescript
import { EntitySchema, EntityRepositoryType } from "@mikro-orm/core";
import { symbol as base } from "@vivalence/runtime";

export class SymbolRepository extends base.repository {
  prune(prefixes: string[], keep: string[]) {
    return this.nativeDelete({ $or: prefixes.map((prefix) => ({ slug: { $like: `${prefix}.%` } })), slug: { $nin: keep } });
  }
}

export class SymbolEntity extends base.entity {
  [EntityRepositoryType]?: SymbolRepository;
}

export const SymbolSchema = new EntitySchema({
  class: SymbolEntity,
  extends: base.schema,
  tableName: "Symbol",
  name: "Symbol",
  repository: () => SymbolRepository,
  properties: {},
});

export default {
  type: "symbol",
  schema: SymbolSchema,
  entity: SymbolEntity,
  repository: SymbolRepository,
};
#+end_src

*** ~/.viva/registry/vcompany/domain/voffice/tools/lookup.js
new. Armed at =/voffice= by the harness (layer ③) — =voffice_lookup=.

#+begin_src javascript
import { v, Vector } from "@vivalence/typology";
import { ONTOLOGY } from "../types.js";
import { line } from "./line.js";

export const lookup = new Vector().open(
  {
    nature: "/lookup",
    valence:
      "Find documents and messages by text — matches slug, document title, mail subject and message body. " +
      "Use whenever unsure an item exists or what its slug is. Nothing matching means it is not in the office: " +
      "say so, never invent a slug.",
    input: v.object({
      text: v.string({ minLength: 1 }).desc("The text to look up."),
      ontology: v.enum(ONTOLOGY).optional().desc("Narrow to one kind."),
      limit: v.integer({ minimum: 1, maximum: 50 }).default(12),
    }),
  },
  async (ctx) => {
    const where = { search: ctx.input.text, ...(ctx.input.ontology && { ontology: ctx.input.ontology }) };
    const literals = await ctx.daemon.entities.literal.find(where, { limit: ctx.input.limit });
    if (!literals.length) return { message: `nothing matches "${ctx.input.text}" — not in the office.` };
    return {
      message: literals.map(line).join("\n"),
      entities: { literal: literals },
      object: { slugs: literals.map((literal) => literal.slug) },
    };
  },
);
#+end_src

*** ~/.viva/registry/vcompany/domain/voffice/tools/line.js
new.

#+begin_src javascript
export const line = (literal) => {
  const { DOCUMENT, MESSAGE, MAIL } = literal.trait ?? {};
  const head = DOCUMENT
    ? `${DOCUMENT.title} · ${DOCUMENT.format} · ${DOCUMENT.readable ? "readable" : "not readable"}`
    : MESSAGE
    ? `${MESSAGE.direction} · ${MESSAGE.from} · ${MAIL?.subject ?? MESSAGE.body.slice(0, 60)}`
    : "";
  return [literal.slug, literal.ontology, head].filter(Boolean).join(" · ");
};
#+end_src

export const aperture = new Aperture().open("/lookup", (input, ctx) =>

    { limit: input.limit ?? 12 },

);

const labeled = (slug, name, description) => ({
  slug,
  traits: ["ONTOLOGICAL", "LABELED"],
  trait: { ONTOLOGICAL: {}, LABELED: { name, description } },
});

const FORMATS = ["md", "org", "json", "txt", "mdx", "jsonc", "pdf", "docx", "xlsx", "tex", "html", "png", "jpg", "svg"];

  {
    slug: "vdex",
    traits: ["ONTOLOGICAL", "LABELED", "TOPOGRAPHICAL"],
    trait: {
      ONTOLOGICAL: {},
      LABELED: { name: "VDex", description: "A document on the JDex mount." },
      TOPOGRAPHICAL: {},
    },
  },
  labeled("vdex.slot.index", "Index", "NN.00 — the category's index."),

];

import paladin from "@vivalence/paladin";
import { v } from "@vivalence/typology";
import { vcompany } from "./daemon.js";

export const manifest = { type: "instance", slug: "vcompany", version: "0.0.1" };

export const runtime = {
  slug: "runtime",
  statics: { serve: () => paladin.env.get("VIVA_RUNTIME_SERVE") },
  datamap: {
    module: "@commons/datamap/libsql",
    statics: { db: { file: `runtime.viva.db` } },
  },
};

export const daemons = [vcompany];

export const clients = {
  kajuit: {
    slug: "kajuit",
    traits: ["ATTACHED"],
    statics: { serve: () => paladin.env.get("VIVA_CLIENT_KAJUIT_SERVE") },
  },
};

export const services = [
  {
    slug: "multiplayer",
    module: "@commons/lighthouse/multiplayer",
    secrets: { jwt: () => paladin.secret.get("SECRET_VIVA_JWT") },
    statics: { serve: () => paladin.env.get("VIVA_LIGHTHOUSE_SERVE") },
    datamap: {
      module: "@commons/datamap/libsql",
      statics: { db: { file: `lighthouse.viva.db` } },
    },
  },
];

export const lighthouse = {
  module: "@commons/lighthouse/multiplayer",
  statics: { remote: () => paladin.env.get("PUBLIC_VIVA_LIGHTHOUSE_REMOTE") },
};

export const environment = v.environment({
  VIVA_RUNTIME_ORIGIN: v.url().desc("Scheme and authority the runtime is reachable at. Every address below derives from it.").default("http://localhost:2501").group("addresses"),
  VIVA_CLIENT_KAJUIT_ORIGIN: v.url().desc("Scheme and authority the kajuit browser client is reachable at.").default("http://localhost:1794").group("addresses"),
  VIVA_RUNTIME_SERVE: v.url().desc("Base URL the runtime serves on. Everything else hangs off this latch.").default("${VIVA_RUNTIME_ORIGIN}/").group("addresses"),
  VIVA_LIGHTHOUSE_SERVE: v.url().desc("Where the hosted lighthouse attaches inside the runtime's own path tree.").default("${VIVA_RUNTIME_ORIGIN}/attached/process/lighthouse/multiplayer").group("addresses"),
  VIVA_CLIENT_KAJUIT_SERVE: v.url().desc("Where the kajuit browser client serves.").default("${VIVA_CLIENT_KAJUIT_ORIGIN}/").group("addresses"),
  PUBLIC_VIVA_RUNTIME_REMOTE: v.url().desc("Runtime address the browser bundle calls. Reaches it through publish(), not a thunk.").default("${VIVA_RUNTIME_SERVE}").group("addresses"),
  PUBLIC_VIVA_LIGHTHOUSE_REMOTE: v.url().desc("Lighthouse address as CONSUMED — by the daemons, and by the browser after publish().").default("${VIVA_LIGHTHOUSE_SERVE}").group("addresses"),
  VIVA_MAIL_IMAP: v.url().desc("imaps://host:993 — the office mailbox.").group("office").optional(),
  VIVA_MAIL_SMTP: v.url().desc("smtps://host:465 — the office outbox.").group("office").optional(),
  SECRET_VIVA_JWT: v.string({ minLength: 24 }).desc("Lighthouse signing secret. Minted at first init; rotate with: openssl rand -base64 24").default(() => btoa(String.fromCharCode(...crypto.getRandomValues(new Uint8Array(24))))).group("keys"),
  SECRET_VIVA_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY: v.string().desc("Anthropic key. Without one every office mode and officer answers as the bot.").group("keys").optional(),
  SECRET_VIVA_MAIL_USER: v.string().desc("Mailbox login.").group("keys").optional(),
  SECRET_VIVA_MAIL_PASSWORD: v.string().desc("Mailbox password.").group("keys").optional(),
  SECRET_VIVA_TELEGRAM_TOKEN: v.string().desc("BotFather token for the office bot.").group("keys").optional(),
});

* T1 — office vdex

Patch-ready. Every repo file carries its hunk against the tree as read this session; every
package file is verbatim; the tests are written. Two of beef's comments landed here: /"NO. no
leaving the mode!"/ (the tools imported the domain's =types.js= by relative path — gone: the
mode owns =types.js= and its own line formatter; nothing under =modes/office/vdex= reaches
outside its directory) and /"not sure hardcoding this is smart. think holistically. what do we
want?"/ (the walker's skip list — answered below: ONE declaration, =belt/ignore.js=, every
walker reads it, every caller extends it).

** entities

*** ontology

=vdex= — the TOPOGRAPHICAL symbol; =Literal.ontology = "vdex"= is DERIVED by the subscriber
now that the link phase runs through the ORM (=known-issues.org= RESOLVED); the boot row still
carries =ontology: "vdex"= explicitly — one truth, twice stated, never in conflict.

*** symbols — scheme (dataset) + coordinates (minted at boot)

#+begin_example
vdex.area.20-29          STRUCTURAL LABELED    name "koerperschaft"   description "20-29_koerperschaft"
vdex.category.22         STRUCTURAL LABELED    name "finance"         description "20-29_koerperschaft/22_finance"
vdex.id.22.04            STRUCTURAL LABELED    name "accounting"      description "20-29_koerperschaft/22_finance/22.04_accounting"
#+end_example

The runtime's symbol fold (=object.set= on dots, =kernel/Literal.ts= subscriber, MEASURED at
=gestalten/belt/object.js:4-13=) keeps the LAST segment as a string value, never as a nested
object — the same shape the dataset test pins for =word.lemma.casa= → ={word: {lemma: "casa"}}=:

#+begin_src javascript
// a filed pdf
literal.symbol === { vdex: { area: "20-29", category: "22", id: { "22": "04" }, format: "pdf" } }
// a category-level file (no id)
literal.symbol === { vdex: { area: "10-19", category: "12", format: "md" } }
// a category index (the NN.00 slot)
literal.symbol === { vdex: { area: "30-39", category: "30", id: { "30": "00" }, slot: "index", format: "mdx" } }
// unfiled — `vdex` alone folds to `true`, the format set replaces it with an object
literal.symbol === { vdex: { format: "org" } }
#+end_src

=index.js= walks exactly this: =symbol.vdex.area= · =.category= · =.id= (one key → =NN.NN=).
Coordinates are OWNED by the vdex mode and pruned with the documents; the scheme rows never are.

The JD index slot names its owner (the docs site's own rule, =documentation/content/jdex.js
indexOf()=): a =NN.00= file directly under an area labels the AREA, under a category labels the
CATEGORY — its =DOCUMENT.title= becomes the coordinate's =LABELED.name=, replacing the folded
directory name. Johnny.Decimal's doctrine says the same thing from the other side: /"The index
is your Johnny.Decimal system"/ — the =00.00= file is the JDex; here the JDex is derived from the
tree and the =NN.00= files hand it their titles.

#+begin_src javascript
{ slug: "vdex.id.22.04", traits: ["STRUCTURAL", "LABELED"],
  trait: { STRUCTURAL: {}, LABELED: { name: "accounting", description: "20-29_koerperschaft/22_finance/22.04_accounting" } } }
#+end_src

*** traits

#+begin_src javascript
DOCUMENT: {
  asset: { path: "20-29_koerperschaft/22_finance/22.04_accounting/Rechnungen/Notariat/RE24-05277.pdf" },   // mount-relative, the truth
  title: "RE24-05277",              // TEXT formats: org #+TITLE › first * · md/mdx `title:` › first # · json title|name · txt first line; else the JD name or the stem
  format: "pdf",                    // lowercased extension
  readable: true,                   // format ∈ statics.formats — the App renders it (pdf through drapes Pdf); tools read TEXT only
  bytes: 48211,
  mtime: "2026-08-12T00:29:00.000Z",
  hash: "b7e1c2d3",                 // hash.string over the decoded bytes (the house fnv1a; no bytes variant exists) — the reindex trigger
}
#+end_src

*** literals — the slug grammar and four rows

#+begin_example
20-29_koerperschaft/22_finance/22.04_accounting/Rechnungen/Notariat/RE24-05277.pdf   →  22.04.re24-05277.pdf
10-19_viva/12_goals/12.04 Ubiquity and a Thin Slice.md                                →  12.04.ubiquity-and-a-thin-slice.md
30-39_architecture/30.00_architecture.mdx                                             →  30.00.architecture.mdx
10-19_viva/12_goals/notes.md                    (category-level file)                 →  12.notes.md
00.00 Index.org                                  (root, the id is the name)           →  00.00.index.org
readme.org                                       (root, no coordinate)                →  readme.org
22.04_accounting/a/x.md · 22.04_accounting/b/x.md                                     →  22.04.x.md · 22.04.x-2.md
#+end_example

A file that carries its own coordinate (=12.04 Ubiquity….md=, =00.00 Index.org=) contributes
its NAME to the slug, not its number — the number is already the prefix. Directories mint
area/category/id symbols; a file mints only an id (a =12 notes.md= file is not a category).

#+begin_src javascript
// a filed PDF — indexed, readable in the App (drapes Pdf), binary to tools
{ slug: "22.04.re24-05277.pdf", ontology: "vdex", traits: ["DOCUMENT"],
  trait: { DOCUMENT: { asset: { path: "20-29_koerperschaft/22_finance/22.04_accounting/Rechnungen/Notariat/RE24-05277.pdf" },
                       title: "RE24-05277", format: "pdf", readable: true, bytes: 48211, mtime: "2026-08-12T00:29:00.000Z", hash: "b7e1c2d3" } },
  symbols: [{ slug: "vdex" }, { slug: "vdex.area.20-29" }, { slug: "vdex.category.22" }, { slug: "vdex.id.22.04" }, { slug: "vdex.format.pdf" }] }

// a readable md file whose id is the file itself
{ slug: "12.04.ubiquity-and-a-thin-slice.md", ontology: "vdex", traits: ["DOCUMENT"],

*** the tool names, by harness

The vdex's own harness slurps =mode.tools= at the armed ROOT: its =/fs/*= collide with paladin's
layer ② and WIN (later wins), so the vdex speaks =fs_read= · =fs_write= · =find= · =open= — every
fs verb rebound to the mountpoint. An officer's AGENTIC harvest branches them under =/vdex=:
=vdex_fs_read= · =vdex_find= · =vdex_open=; the branch carries vdex's own binds (=ctx.mode= =
vdex, =ctx.root= = the JDex — =population.js:128= binds the mode on =mode.tools= itself, and a
slurped carry rides into the branch), so the officer's =ctx.mode= never leaks in.

*** the ignore doctrine — beef's /"think holistically. what do we want?"/ · the connoisseur's pass

What exists today: THREE hardcoded lists — =belt/find.js:11= (=bak= · =archive= · =slp= ·
=*.bak=, directories only), =skills/fs.js:4= (=bak= · =archive= · =slp= · =node_modules= ·
=.git=), and the vdex's own =statics.ignore= — plus =freight.test.js= pinning that =bak= is
skipped. What we want: ONE declaration of the house names, every walker reading it, every
caller extending it for its own root, and the instance extending it through the mask.

- =subsystems/paladin/belt/ignore.js= — =IGNORE=, a glob list (=*.bak= in it as data, the suffix rule the walker used to hardcode) + =skip(globs = IGNORE)=, compiled once into a name predicate.

- a caller's list is the WHOLE list, defaulted to the declaration — no union, no "extras": FRAUGHT passes =mode.statics?.ignore=, the vdex loader passes =mode.statics.ignore=, the vdex tools bind =ctx.ignore= beside =ctx.root= so an officer's =vdex_fs_tree= and the index agree.
- the instance's kernel entry =statics.ignore= (=daemon.js=) therefore carries the house names + =*.bak= + =jdex-sbs= — beef's entry already did, one glob joins it.
- NOT a gitignore-style file in the walked tree: index-ignore ≠ vcs-ignore (=.env= is ignored by git and absent from the index anyway; =jdex-sbs= is committed and must NOT be indexed). Declared, not discovered.
- the connoisseur's pass on the first cut (his /"something is off"/) — five findings, above the =ignore.js= block in =** files=.

import { App, Dataset, Freight, v } from "@vivalence/typology";
import { coordinates, documents, fingerprint } from "./load.js";
export { boot } from "./boot.js";
export { aperture } from "./aperture/index.js";
export { tools } from "./tools/index.js";
export { harness } from "./harness.js";

export const manifest = {
  type: "office",
  slug: "vdex",
  name: "VDex",
  version: "0.1.0",
  description:
    "The JDex on disk as literals: the tree is the literal source, boot prunes what is gone and mints the coordinates; " +
    "tools and the app read and write through the same paladin verbs.",

};

});

);

*** ~/.viva/registry/vcompany/modes/office/vdex/jd.js
new. Both grammars on disk (=NN-NN_name= · =NN-NN name=); directories mint area · category · id,
a file mints only its own id; the deepest coordinate is the slug prefix, the extension is kept.

#+begin_src javascript
const AREA = /^(\d\d)-(\d\d)[ _](.+)$/;
const CATEGORY = /^(\d\d)[ _](.+)$/;
const ID = /^(\d\d)\.(\d\d)[ _](.+?)(\.[^.]+)?$/;
const SLOT = { "00": "index" };

export const fold = (text) =>
  text
    .normalize("NFKD")
    .replace(/[\u0300-\u036f]/g, "")
    .toLowerCase()
    .replace(/[^a-z0-9]+/g, "-")
    .replace(/^-|-$/g, "");

const structural = (slug, name, description) => ({
  slug,
  traits: ["STRUCTURAL", "LABELED"],
  trait: { STRUCTURAL: {}, LABELED: { name: fold(name).replaceAll("-", " "), description } },
});

export const coordinate = (id) =>
  id.includes(".") ? `vdex.id.${id}` : id.includes("-") ? `vdex.area.${id}` : `vdex.category.${id}`;

export function coordinates(path) {
  const symbols = [];
  const segments = path.split("/");
  let prefix = "";
  let name = null;
  segments.forEach((segment, index) => {
    const here = segments.slice(0, index + 1).join("/");
    const file = index === segments.length - 1;
    let match;
    if ((match = segment.match(ID))) {
      symbols.push(structural(`vdex.id.${match[1]}.${match[2]}`, match[3], here));
      if (SLOT[match[2]]) symbols.push({ slug: `vdex.slot.${SLOT[match[2]]}` });
      prefix = `${match[1]}.${match[2]}`;
      if (file) name = match[3];
    } else if (!file && (match = segment.match(AREA))) {
      symbols.push(structural(`vdex.area.${match[1]}-${match[2]}`, match[3], here));
      prefix = `${match[1]}-${match[2]}`;
    } else if (!file && (match = segment.match(CATEGORY))) {
      symbols.push(structural(`vdex.category.${match[1]}`, match[2], here));
      prefix = match[1];
    }
  });
  return { symbols, prefix, name };
}

export const slugOf = (jd, file, seen) => {
  const filename = file.path.split("/").pop();
  const dot = filename.lastIndexOf(".");
  const ext = dot > 0 ? filename.slice(dot + 1).toLowerCase() : "";
  const stem = fold(jd.name ?? (dot > 0 ? filename.slice(0, dot) : filename));
  const base = [jd.prefix, stem].filter(Boolean).join(".");
  let slug = ext ? `${base}.${ext}` : base;
  for (let n = 2; seen.has(slug); n++) slug = ext ? `${base}-${n}.${ext}` : `${base}-${n}`;
  seen.add(slug);
  return slug;
};
#+end_src

*** ~/.viva/registry/vcompany/modes/office/vdex/title.js
new. Pure over the text the loader already holds (its first 4 kB); falls back to what the caller
hands it (the JD name or the stem). Nothing is opened twice.

#+begin_src javascript
const HEAD = 4096;

const BY = {
  org: [/^#\+TITLE:\s*(.+)$/im, /^\*+\s+(.+)$/m],
  md: [/^title:\s*["']?(.+?)["']?\s*$/m, /^#\s+(.+)$/m],
  mdx: [/^title:\s*["']?(.+?)["']?\s*$/m, /^#\s+(.+)$/m],
  json: [/"(?:title|name)"\s*:\s*"([^"]+)"/],
  jsonc: [/"(?:title|name)"\s*:\s*"([^"]+)"/],
  txt: [/^\s*(\S.*)$/m],
};

export const title = (text, format, fallback) => {
  const head = text.slice(0, HEAD);
  for (const expression of BY[format] ?? []) {
    const match = head.match(expression);
    if (match) return match[1].trim();
  }
  return fallback;
};
#+end_src

*** ~/.viva/registry/vcompany/modes/office/vdex/load.js
new. The two loaders and their stamp — the vdex's =reader.load= sources. ONE scan per install feeds
both: the symbol source runs first (=Dataset= keeps declaration order), the literal source shares
the memo, BOOTED drains it for the prune. =fingerprint= is a stat-only =find.index=: nothing is read
on an unchanged tree. The content hash is computed here, by the reader that holds the text (a
binary format hashes bytes + mtime); =title= reads the same text.

#+begin_src javascript
import { parse } from "@std/path";
import paladin from "@vivalence/paladin";
import { hash } from "@vivalence/typology";
import { TEXT } from "./types.js";
import { coordinates as jd, slugOf } from "./jd.js";
import { title } from "./title.js";

const scans = new WeakMap();

export const row = async (mode, file, coordinate, seen) => {
  const stem = coordinate.name ?? parse(file.path).name;
  const text = TEXT.includes(file.format) ? await Deno.readTextFile(`${mode.mountpoint.absolute}/${file.path}`) : null;
  return {
    slug: slugOf(coordinate, file, seen),
    ontology: "vdex",
    traits: ["DOCUMENT"],
    trait: {
      DOCUMENT: {
        asset: { path: file.path },
        title: text === null ? stem : title(text, file.format, stem),
        format: file.format,
        readable: mode.statics.formats.includes(file.format),
        bytes: file.bytes,
        mtime: file.mtime,
        hash: hash.string(text ?? `${file.bytes}:${file.mtime}`),
      },
    },
    symbols: [{ slug: "vdex" }, ...coordinate.symbols.map(({ slug }) => ({ slug })), { slug: `vdex.format.${file.format}` }],
  };
};

// NN.00 names its owner — the category it sits in, or the area when it sits directly under one.
const label = (minted, coordinate, named) => {
  if (!coordinate.symbols.some((symbol) => symbol.slug === "vdex.slot.index")) return;
  const owner = coordinate.symbols.filter((symbol) => symbol.traits).at(-2);
  if (owner) minted.get(owner.slug).trait.LABELED.name = named;
};

export const scan = async (mode) => {
  const files = await paladin.find.index(mode.mountpoint.absolute, mode.statics.ignore);
  const seen = new Set();
  const minted = new Map();
  const literal = [];
  for (const file of files) {
    const coordinate = jd(file.path);
    for (const symbol of coordinate.symbols) if (symbol.traits && !minted.has(symbol.slug)) minted.set(symbol.slug, symbol);
    const held = await row(mode, file, coordinate, seen);
    literal.push(held);
    label(minted, coordinate, held.trait.DOCUMENT.title);
  }
  return { symbol: [...minted.values()], literal };
};

const scanned = (mode) => scans.get(mode) ?? scans.set(mode, scan(mode)).get(mode);

export const coordinates = async (mode) => (await scanned(mode)).symbol;

export const documents = async (mode) => (await scanned(mode)).literal;

export const drain = async (mode) => {
  const held = scans.get(mode);
  scans.delete(mode);
  return held && (await held);
};

export const fingerprint = async (mode) =>
  hash.array((await paladin.find.index(mode.mountpoint.absolute, mode.statics.ignore)).map(({ path, bytes, mtime }) => [path, bytes, mtime]));
#+end_src

*** ~/.viva/registry/vcompany/modes/office/vdex/boot.js
new. BOOTED: =boot(daemon, mode)= after every trait is wired — DATASET has pulled the loaders by
then (same mode, =stagger= awaits it) or skipped them (stamp unmoved, no scan to drain). BOOTED
drains the scan when there is one and prunes by ontology and prefix on the voffice repositories (no
owner rows needed), then prints the line from the database; returns nothing (no teardown in v1).
=reindex= after an own write is the same install — blank the stamp in memory, run the trait, drain,
admit to the freight, prune — one way, the whole tree: a JDex is small and an unchanged row patches
to nothing. His WEIRD is what this replaces: nothing of the trait is exported or split.

#+begin_src javascript
import * as runtime from "@vivalence/runtime";
import { drain } from "./load.js";

const COORDINATES = ["vdex.area", "vdex.category", "vdex.id"];

const prune = async (daemon, fresh) => ({
  literal: await daemon.entities.literal.prune("vdex", fresh.literal.map((held) => held.slug)),
  symbol: await daemon.entities.symbol.prune(COORDINATES, fresh.symbol.map((held) => held.slug)),
});

const line = async (daemon, mode, pruned) =>
  console.log(
    `[VDEX] ${mode.mountpoint.absolute} ${await daemon.entities.literal.count({ ontology: "vdex" })} documents · ` +
      `${await daemon.entities.symbol.count({ $or: COORDINATES.map((prefix) => ({ slug: { $like: `${prefix}.%` } })) })} coordinates · ` +
      `pruned ${pruned.literal}/${pruned.symbol}`,
  );

export async function boot(daemon, mode) {
  const fresh = await drain(mode);
  await line(daemon, mode, fresh ? await prune(daemon, fresh) : { literal: 0, symbol: 0 });
}

export const reindex = async (daemon, mode) => {
  mode.entity.installed = "";
  await runtime.daemon.traits.DATASET(mode, daemon);
  const fresh = await drain(mode);
  for (const held of fresh.literal) mode.freight.admit(held.trait.DOCUMENT.asset.path);
  const pruned = await prune(daemon, fresh);
  await line(daemon, mode, pruned);
  return pruned;
};
#+end_src

*** ~/.viva/registry/vcompany/modes/office/vdex/index.js
new. The JDex as text — ONE literal query (=fields: ["slug", "symbol", "trait"]=) grouped by the
folded =symbol.vdex= tree, ONE symbol query for the LABELED names; counts and format tallies per
id; over budget the depth folds (ids → category counts → area counts). Deterministic order.

#+begin_src javascript
const tally = (formats) =>
  Object.entries(formats)
    .sort(([a], [b]) => (a < b ? -1 : 1))
    .map(([format, count]) => (count > 1 ? `${format} ×${count}` : format))
    .join(", ");

const named = (labels, slug, key) => `${key} ${labels.get(slug) ?? ""}`.trim();

const fold = (areas) => {
  const folded = {};
  for (const [area, categories] of Object.entries(areas)) {
    const slot = (folded[area] = { count: 0, formats: {} });
    for (const ids of Object.values(categories))
      for (const { count, formats } of Object.values(ids)) {
        slot.count += count;
        for (const [format, n] of Object.entries(formats)) slot.formats[format] = (slot.formats[format] ?? 0) + n;
      }
  }
  return folded;
};

const render = (mount, areas, labels, unfiled, depth) => {
  const lines = [`[VDex — ${mount}]`];
  for (const area of Object.keys(areas).sort()) {
    const categories = areas[area];
    if (depth === 1) {
      const { count, formats } = fold({ [area]: categories })[area];
      lines.push(`${named(labels, `vdex.area.${area}`, area)} · ${count} · ${tally(formats)}`);
      continue;
    }
    lines.push(named(labels, `vdex.area.${area}`, area));
    for (const category of Object.keys(categories).sort()) {
      const ids = categories[category];
      const indent = category ? "  " : "";
      if (category && depth === 2) {
        const { count, formats } = fold({ [area]: { [category]: ids } })[area];
        lines.push(`  ${named(labels, `vdex.category.${category}`, category)} · ${count} · ${tally(formats)}`);
        continue;
      }
      if (category) lines.push(`  ${named(labels, `vdex.category.${category}`, category)}`);
      for (const id of Object.keys(ids).sort()) {
        const { count, formats } = ids[id];
        const head = id ? named(labels, `vdex.id.${id}`, id) : "(loose)";
        lines.push(`${indent}  ${head} · ${count} · ${tally(formats)}`);
      }
    }
  }
  lines.push(`unfiled · ${unfiled}`);
  return lines.join("\n");
};

export const index = async (daemon, mode, { under = "", budget = 6000 } = {}) => {
  const symbols = await daemon.entities.symbol.find({ slug: { $like: "vdex.%" } });
  const labels = new Map(symbols.map((symbol) => [symbol.slug, symbol.trait?.LABELED?.name ?? ""]));
  const literals = await daemon.entities.literal.find({ ontology: "vdex" }, { fields: ["slug", "symbol", "trait"] });
  const areas = {};
  let unfiled = 0;
  for (const literal of literals) {
    const at = literal.symbol?.vdex;
    if (!at?.area) {
      unfiled++;
      continue;
    }
    const category = at.category ?? "";
    const id = at.id ? `${Object.keys(at.id)[0]}.${Object.values(at.id)[0]}` : "";
    if (under && ![at.area, category, id].some((key) => key.startsWith(under))) continue;
    const slot = (((areas[at.area] ??= {})[category] ??= {})[id] ??= { count: 0, formats: {} });
    slot.count++;
    const format = literal.trait?.DOCUMENT?.format ?? "";
    slot.formats[format] = (slot.formats[format] ?? 0) + 1;
  }
  for (const depth of [3, 2, 1]) {
    const text = render(mode.mountpoint.absolute, areas, labels, unfiled, depth);
    if (text.length <= budget || depth === 1) return text;
  }
};
#+end_src

*** ~/.viva/registry/vcompany/modes/office/vdex/harness.js

export const harness = new Vector().use(async (ctx, next) => {
  ctx.hallucination.system.vdex = await index(ctx.daemon, ctx.mode);
  await next();

*** ~/.viva/registry/vcompany/modes/office/vdex/tools/index.js
new. paladin's fs verbs with =root= REBOUND to the mountpoint (later-wins over the harness's
module-root arming, =traits/harnessed.js:43-46=) and =ignore= bound beside it (the skill compiles
it per call, so an officer's =vdex_fs_tree= and the index agree), a guard that keeps =fs/read= to
TEXT formats, a reindex after every own write, and two vdex verbs. MEASURED: a =.use= middleware short-circuits
by setting =ctx.output= and not calling =next= — =steer/strategy.js resolve()= only reads the
EFFECT's return, a middleware's return value is dropped (=belt/middleware.js compose=); the
envelope is the m26 yield contract ={condition?, output: {message?, object?, <entity>[]}}=,
entity keys SINGULAR (=literal= · =buffer=); =fromm.yield= lifts the rest. Nothing here imports
from outside the mode.

#+begin_src javascript
import { extname } from "@std/path";
import { v, Vector } from "@vivalence/typology";
import paladin from "@vivalence/paladin";
import { TEXT } from "../types.js";
import { coordinate } from "../jd.js";
import { reindex } from "../boot.js";

const format = (path = "") => extname(path).slice(1).toLowerCase();
const verb = (ctx) => ctx.signal?.pathname?.match(/\/fs\/(\w+)$/)?.[1];
const line = (literal) => {
  const { title, format, readable } = literal.trait.DOCUMENT;
  return `${literal.slug} · ${title} · ${format}${readable ? "" : " · not readable"}`;
};

export const tools = new Vector()
  .use(async (ctx, next) => {
    ctx.root = ctx.mode.mountpoint.absolute;
    ctx.ignore = ctx.mode.statics.ignore;
    if (verb(ctx) === "read" && !TEXT.includes(format(ctx.input.path))) {
      const cargo = ctx.mode.freight.catalog[ctx.input.path];
      ctx.output = {
        output: {
          message:
            `${ctx.input.path} is ${format(ctx.input.path) || "extensionless"} — binary; ` +
            `open it in the app: ${cargo?.url ?? "not in the cargo"}`,
        },
      };
      return;
    }
    await next();
    if (["write", "move", "delete"].includes(verb(ctx))) await reindex(ctx.daemon, ctx.mode);
  })
  .slurp(paladin.skills.fs)
  .open(
    {
      nature: "/find",
      valence:
        "Find documents by JD coordinate, title or slug. The harness already shows the index — resolve a coordinate " +
        "to its documents before reading. Binary formats (pdf, images) are listed and opened in the app, never read.",
      input: v.object({
        id: v.string().optional().desc("A JD coordinate: 22, 22.04, 20-29."),
        text: v.string().optional().desc("Matches slug and title."),
        limit: v.integer({ minimum: 1, maximum: 50 }).default(20),
      }),
    },
    async (ctx) => {
      const where = { ontology: "vdex" };
      if (ctx.input.id) where.symbols = [coordinate(ctx.input.id)];
      if (ctx.input.text) where.search = ctx.input.text;
      const literals = await ctx.daemon.entities.literal.find(where, { limit: ctx.input.limit });
      return { output: { message: literals.map(line).join("\n") || "nothing filed there.", literal: literals } };
    },
  )
  .open(
    {
      nature: "/open",
      valence: "Put a document on the operator's screen — the reader opens it in the VDex app.",
      input: v.object({ slug: v.string() }),
    },
    async (ctx) => {
      const literal = await ctx.daemon.entities.literal.findOne({ ontology: "vdex", slug: ctx.input.slug });
      if (!literal) return { condition: "ERROR", output: { message: `${ctx.input.slug} is not in the vdex` } };
      const buffer = await ctx.mode.app.buffer({ thread: ctx.thread, literals: [literal.id], data: { open: literal.slug } });
      await ctx.daemon.entities.em.flush();
      return { output: { message: `${literal.slug} on screen.`, buffer: [buffer] } };
    },
  );
#+end_src

*** ~/.viva/registry/vcompany/modes/office/vdex/aperture/index.js
new. What the App calls (=mode.call=, EXPOSED — tools are not reachable from Svelte) and what an
officer reads. =resolve= is the fs skill's root-bound resolver, exported in T1.b — nothing
escapes the mountpoint from either side. Handlers take =(input, ctx)= (=strategy.js fire()=,
arity 2).

#+begin_src javascript
import { Aperture } from "@vivalence/typology";
import paladin from "@vivalence/paladin";
import { TEXT } from "../types.js";
import { coordinate } from "../jd.js";
import { index } from "../index.js";
import { reindex } from "../boot.js";

const COORDINATE = /^vdex\.(area|category|id)\./;
const document = (ctx, slug) => ctx.daemon.entities.literal.findOne({ ontology: "vdex", slug });
const at = (ctx, path) => paladin.skills.resolve(ctx.mode.mountpoint.absolute, path);

export const aperture = new Aperture()
  .open("/index", (input, ctx) => index(ctx.daemon, ctx.mode, input))
  .open("/tree", async (input, ctx) =>
    (await ctx.daemon.entities.symbol.find({ slug: { $like: "vdex.%" } })).filter((symbol) => COORDINATE.test(symbol.slug)),
  )
  .open("/documents", (input, ctx) =>
    ctx.daemon.entities.literal.find(
      { ontology: "vdex", ...(input.id && { symbols: [coordinate(input.id)] }) },
      { limit: input.limit ?? 50 },
    ),
  )
  .open("/document", async (input, ctx) => {
    const literal = await document(ctx, input.slug);
    if (!literal) return null;
    const { asset, format } = literal.trait.DOCUMENT;
    return {
      literal,
      url: ctx.mode.freight.catalog[asset.path]?.url ?? null,
      text: TEXT.includes(format) ? await Deno.readTextFile(at(ctx, asset.path)) : null,
    };
  })
  .open("/write", async (input, ctx) => {
    const literal = await document(ctx, input.slug);
    if (!literal) throw new Error(`${input.slug} is not in the vdex`);
    await Deno.writeTextFile(at(ctx, literal.trait.DOCUMENT.asset.path), input.text);
    await reindex(ctx.daemon, ctx.mode);
    return document(ctx, input.slug);
  });
#+end_src

*** ~/.viva/registry/vcompany/modes/office/vdex/buffer/Vdex.svelte
REPLACED by the screens App on /"go"/ (s2 — the landed file is the block under =* screens=). The T1 stub as it was, the one beef screenshotted (/"lol."/):
new. Coordinates › documents › reader › Textarea; everything through =mode.call= (the aperture
above); the five kajuit view props. Opens =buffer.data.open= when the buffer came from =vdex_open=.

#+begin_src svelte
<script>
  import { Card, Header, Paragraph, Textarea, Button, Markdown, Org, Json, Pdf } from "@vivalence/drapes";

  let { terminal, daemon, mode, thread, buffer } = $props();

  let coordinates = $state([]);
  let documents = $state([]);
  let open = $state(null);
  let text = $state("");

  const name = (symbol) => `${symbol.slug.split(".").slice(2).join(".")} ${symbol.trait?.LABELED?.name ?? ""}`.trim();

  async function list(id) {
    documents = await mode.call.documents({ id });
  }
  async function read(slug) {
    open = await mode.call.document({ slug });
    text = open?.text ?? "";
  }
  async function save() {
    open = await mode.call.write({ slug: open.literal.slug, text });
  }
  async function load() {
    coordinates = await mode.call.tree({});
    if (buffer?.data?.open) await read(buffer.data.open);
  }
  load();
</script>

<div class="vdex">
  <Card>
    <Header>JDex</Header>
    {#each coordinates as symbol (symbol.slug)}
      <button type="button" onclick={() => list(symbol.slug.split(".").slice(2).join("."))}>{name(symbol)}</button>
    {/each}
  </Card>
  <Card>
    <Header>documents</Header>
    {#each documents as literal (literal.slug)}
      <button type="button" onclick={() => read(literal.slug)}>{literal.trait.DOCUMENT.title} · {literal.trait.DOCUMENT.format}</button>
    {/each}
  </Card>
  <Card>
    {#if open}
      {@const { format, readable, title } = open.literal.trait.DOCUMENT}
      <Header>{title}</Header>
      {#if !readable}
        <Paragraph>{format} — not readable here.</Paragraph>
      {:else if format === "pdf"}
        <Pdf url={open.url} />
      {:else}
        {#if format === "md" || format === "mdx"}<Markdown {text} />
        {:else if format === "org"}<Org {text} />
        {:else if format === "json" || format === "jsonc"}<Json value={JSON.parse(text || "null")} />
        {:else}<Paragraph>{text}</Paragraph>{/if}
        <Textarea bind:value={text} />
        <Button onclick={save}>save</Button>
      {/if}
    {/if}
  </Card>
</div>

<style>
  .vdex {
    display: grid;
    grid-template-columns: 1fr 1fr 2fr;
    gap: 8px;
    height: 100%;
  }
  button {
    display: block;
    background: transparent;
    border: none;
    color: inherit;
    font: inherit;
    text-align: left;
    cursor: pointer;
    padding: 2px 0;
  }
</style>
#+end_src
