** the rules (System §07 · Screens M) — the contract every App keeps
- space: slugs · addresses · coordinates · tool names · states in code, lowercase, tracked; titles · bodies · what an officer says in sans; never both in one span.
- colour: primary marks the ONE active thing per panel (tick · tab · row · pip); signal colours mark state, never structure; the zone ladder carries depth (list = skeleton 2, cards = 3, page = 0).
- motion: one pulse (pip 1.8 s, drapes has it), one heartbeat (the spine), one caret; hover changes colour at 0.12–0.16 s; nothing slides — the vdex sheet is the one exception the design draws (M2), 0.16 s.
- the shell does the layout; an App never draws chrome or nav; =narrow= = the App's own width < 820 (the Dock's measure), then list › detail with a 44 px ‹ key; =(pointer: coarse)= → rows ≥ 44, keys 44, chips 36, send 34; safe areas are the shell's (=client.html=); type does not shrink; long slugs ellipsize, prose wraps.

** blast radius

| layer   | files                                                                      | tier |
|---------+----------------------------------------------------------------------------+------|
| drapes  | controls/Key · display/{Tile,Plate,Entry,Empty} · Pip +glow · two barrels   | b2   |
| package | vdex {vdex.viva.js buffer schema · aperture /open · buffer/Vdex.svelte}    | T1   |
| package | email {aperture · buffer/Inbox.svelte} · chat {aperture · buffer/Chat.svelte} | T2 |
| package | officer/ceo {ceo.viva.js APPLICATION · buffer/Desk.svelte}                 | T5 (frame now) |
| package | domain/voffice {domain.viva.js APPLICATION · buffer/Voffice.svelte}        | T0+  |
| package | office/calendar (new mode)                                                 | CALL |
| kajuit  | panels/a/widgets/Dock.svelte header                                        | T5   |

** gates
- Gs1 drapes — compile gate LANDED (=parts.test.js= 6 parts + barrels green); the render is his click-through: the five parts render in a scratch view under =nordic= and =paper=; =Pip glow= on an unread row; nothing in kajuit changes (the Dock still imports its own shapes).
- Gs2 vdex App — compile gate + =/open= snapshot LANDED (=app.test.js= · =vdex-aperture.snapshot.json= carries =open= with its literal); the click-through is his after a restart: open the vdex → the tree lists the fixture JDex with counts → a md reads → edit → save → disk changes → =reindex= keeps the slug and moves the hash; a pdf opens through =Pdf= with the =binary= line; the find box narrows to slugs; under 820 px the tree is a sheet and the ‹ key opens it.
- Gs3 voffice App (T0 modules + drapes): officers three plates, seven ghosts; offices four cards + the ledger slot; counts match =[VDEX]= · =[MAIL]= lines.
- Gs4 desk (frame): three tiles from the queries, the inbox top three jump into the mail App, a recent document jumps into the vdex — the cross-App jump is the GATE ITEM (=terminal.buffer= from a server-minted buffer).
- Gs5 mail + chat (T2): the inbox lists · filters · the preview marks read · a reply lands as an out row with =uses= and the parent flips to answered; a chat sends and the row appears; both list › detail under 820 px.
- Gs6 dock header (T5): the CEO plate in the dock header; keyed vs bot follows the cortex.
- not measured: =find= =orderBy= through the remote repository · =mode.statics= on the client Mode · =daemon.metadata.company= · the =context= console's contents · a server-minted buffer's arrival in =daemon.entities.buffer=.

* V3 — the vdex App pass (surface · functionality · later)

Worked out whole in the sibling file =m53-vcompany.v3-vdex.org= (this file was under another session's pen): 11 package files LANDED on his go, instance suite green (8 passed · 42 steps), restart + click-through his; LATER list marked there; ONE delta owed to T1.1 =placement= (rename in place) and ONE drapes line (=Pip= tone =success=).

** LATER — the six the V3 pass marked, why each waits and where it lands

His order at the V3 pass: /"everything up to the upload, download etc"/, then these six MARKED here in the
root. Each row is the seam, not a plan; the design draws all six (=V3 office-vdex.dc.html=).

| item                  | why it waits                                                              | where it lands                                          |
|-----------------------+---------------------------------------------------------------------------+---------------------------------------------------------|
| =← in= backlinks      | nothing carries what a document points AT                                 | =load.js= trait mint → =Outline.svelte= links tab       |
| =→ N= on rows         | same trait gap; the App holds text for ONE open document                  | =load.js= trait mint → =Rows.svelte= meta line          |
| link editing          | no target picker, no insertion point the block surface can track          | =Reader.svelte= =wrap()= + the find fold                |
| ocr                   | no service; a pdf's text is not on the mount                              | an office service, then =load.js= stores what it yields |
| ↗ ask                 | the officers are OUT (his ruling); nothing to jump into                   | =terminal.buffer= cross-App jump (the Gs4 gate item)    |
| reindex               | =boot.js= exports =boot · sync · watch= only; no route to call            | =aperture/index.js= one =.open("/reindex", …)=          |

*** =← in= backlinks and =→ N= on rows — one trait field carries both

MEASURED: a row is a =literal= whose =trait.DOCUMENT= is =asset.path · title · format · readable · bytes ·
mtime · hash= (=load.js:18-26=). Nothing in it says what the document REFERENCES. The App sees references only
in text it has already fetched, and =/document= serves ONE document's text — so with 33 rows and one document
open it can count that document's forward refs and nothing else. Backwards (=← in=) is worse: it would need
every document's text at once, i.e. 33 calls at every open.

WHERE: =load.js= is the one place that already holds the bytes — it reads the file at =load.js:12= to take the
title and the hash. The mint grows one field, =uses: string[]=, the coordinates the reference grammar finds in
that text. Then both items are free reads on rows the App already has:
- =→ N= is =Rows.svelte='s meta line, beside =size · age= — the design writes =2 kB · 2mo · → 1=.
- =← in= is =Outline.svelte='s links tab: =documents.filter((held) => document(held).uses.includes(coordinate))=,
  no call, no fetch. Its Empty ("backlinks · later") comes out.

CAREFUL: the reference grammar would then live twice — =buffer/jdex.js= =REFERENCE= (the client, for the
reader's jumps) and the daemon's mint. They must be ONE module in the package, or a document's arrows will
mean different things on the two sides. =/write= already reindexes the file it wrote, so =uses= stays fresh
without a pass; a document that references a coordinate MINTED LATER stays stale until either side is touched
— boot's =sync= is the sweeper.

*** link editing — the toolbar's =→ link= is drawn, disabled

MEASURED: the block toolbar (=Reader.svelte= =.tools=) carries =B I `= (real, they wrap the selection),
=H2 LIST ¶= (real, they convert through =parse= → =compose=), =×= (real, it drops the block) and =→ link=
(disabled). The design's own reference has NO handler on =→ link= either.

WHY: writing a link is two things the surface does not have. A TARGET — the picker is the find fold, which
today lives in the header and answers =id: · format: · text=; it would have to open under the caret and
answer with a coordinate. An INSERTION POINT — a DOM Range inside a contentEditable, kept across the commit
that recomposes the section. =wrap()= is the seam: it already inserts a text node at the selection and
commits. =jdex.linked()= already renders =→ NN.NN= as a jump in the format's own grammar, so nothing new is
needed to READ what would be written.

RIDES: the =uses= field above — an edited link should move the outline in the same breath.

*** ocr — no service to ask

MEASURED: a pdf's =trait.DOCUMENT.readable= is =false= and =/document= serves =text: null= (=aperture/index.js=
=serve()= reads text only for =TEXT= formats). The chip says =ocr · later= because there is no faculty to hand
the bytes to.

WHERE: an office service (the T2 shape — vendor services with a shared belt), called behind the harness, then
=load.js= stores what it yields: either a sibling text file on the mount (the JD way — it becomes a row of its
own) or a trait field on the pdf's literal. Which one is a real fork and beef's: a sibling file is visible,
greppable and survives a reindex; a trait field keeps one row per document.

*** ↗ ask — the officers are out

His ruling at T1.1: /"ignore the officers for now"/. The chip would open a CEO thread with the document as its
context. That is the SAME move as Gs4's gate item — a cross-App jump through =terminal.buffer= from a served
buffer — so it lands with the officer stage, not before, and it is one line in =Reader.svelte='s chips when it
does.

*** reindex — the route is missing, not the work

MEASURED: =boot.js= exports =boot=, =sync= and =watch= (=boot.js:61 · 151 · 155=). There is NO =reindex=
export, and the aperture's eight routes (=/index /tree /documents /document /write /file /move /delete=) do
not include one. A stale test still imports =reindex= from =boot.js= and fails the suite red — see the
sibling's landing below.

WHERE: =sync(daemon, mode, paths)= already does the work, per path, serialized on its own chain. The route is
one line in =aperture/index.js= over it, and =Browser.svelte='s menu entry drops its =later= and calls it.
The only real question is its INPUT: the whole mount (a full pass, what the design's menu means) or a path
list (what =sync= takes). Beef's call.

** the pdf never displays — the cargo route drops any path with a space

MEASURED in the live app this session, not read: the vdex serves =open.url= from
=ctx.mode.freight.catalog[asset.path].url=, and the catalog is right — 33 entries, each with its url. The
route is mounted (=/attached/cargo/daemon/vcompany/(.*)= is in =/metadata/aperture=). Two fetches from the
page, same directory, same daemon:

| path                                                        | answer                                  |
|-------------------------------------------------------------+-----------------------------------------|
| =20.01_corporation/Gesellschafterliste.pdf=                 | 200 · =application/pdf= · 481 262 bytes |
| =20.01_corporation/Beherrschungs- und Gewinnabführungs….pdf= | 404 · =application/json= · =null=       |

The difference is the SPACE. The url is minted with the raw path (=Freight.withUrl=, =FRAUGHT= in
=systems/runtime/daemon/traits/index.js:47=), the browser percent-encodes it on the wire, and the handler at
=systems/runtime/lifecycle/resolve.js:53-56= matches the capture against the catalog WITHOUT decoding it —
=fromm.params(ctx.params).path.absolute= still carries =%20=, =mode.freight.resolve(query)= misses, every mode
misses, the handler falls through and the aperture answers 404 =null=. Auth is not it: the plain path serves
with no bearer at all.

So every document whose filename carries a space — most of his =koerperschaft= pdfs — has an unreachable
viewer. =↗ cargo= opens the same dead url in a tab.

FIX (=systems/=, GATED — one seam, two honest halves): decode the capture before resolving, and mint the url
percent-encoded per segment so the two sides agree. Until it lands the App is HONEST rather than blank: the
viewer fetches the url first and, when it cannot, draws =cargo unreachable · the cargo route answered 404 ·
<the url>= instead of a white sheet (=Reader.svelte=, landed).

** two mounts of one module — mask.slug · the owner relation · scope by mode (LANDED on his go)

His kernel now mounts =@vcompany/office/vdex= twice, =vdocs= on the documentation content and =corpo= on the
company mount. Four things stood between that config and a working daemon; all four are landed, uncommitted.

*** the slug override was dropped on the floor

=Mode= takes =this.slug = this.manifest.slug=, and =population.js= read =mask.statics=, =mask.secrets= and
=mask.mountpoint= off the kernel entry but never =mask.slug=. Both entries came up as =vdex=, both computed
=mode.mount= as =/mode/office/vdex=, and =modes[type][slug]= registered one over the other — one mode, on
whichever mountpoint loaded last. Now the declaration's slug names THIS mounting and the manifest's names the
module; the mode row is ensured on the effective slug, so =office/vdocs= and =office/corpo= are two rows.

*** the ownership relation he designed in =m53-vcompany.pre.org= — landed, SINGLE owner

His sketch there was m:n both ways. His ruling at the landing: /"just one … one mode per literal/symbol"/.
So =Literal.mode= and =Symbol.mode= are =m:1= (nullable), =Mode.literals= and =Mode.symbols= are =1:m=, and
the schematics mirror it. The unique key on both moved from =slug= to =(slug, mode)= — without that the two
mounts could not both hold =32.01=, because a literal's slug is the identity every verb uses.

*** the ontology's vocabulary is shared; what a mount reads is owned

The first cut owned everything a mode installed and it crashed his runtime:
/"Literal "10.00.about.mdx" must have exactly one TOPOGRAPHICAL symbol, found 2"/. The =vdex= symbol is the
ONTOLOGY marker — one ontology, however many mounts carry it — and scoping it per mode minted a second one,
so a document ended up carrying two. The rule now: the declared scheme (=dataset/symbols.js= — the ontology
marker, the formats, the slots) is shared and unowned, because it is the ontology's vocabulary and the
ontology does not belong to a mount; what the mode COMPUTES (=load=) is that mounting's own reading of its
world and is owned. The vdex's boot follows the same rule — a coordinate is looked up strictly per mode, a scheme
symbol strictly unowned, no fallback either way.

*** every read scoped

=boot.js= (rows · structural · orphans · prune · report · upsert · reconcile), =index.js=, the aperture
(=/tree= =/documents= =/document=, and =home()=), the tools, the harness, the emitter, and the App's two
subscriptions. Prune is now the mounting's own: =corpo= booting no longer deletes =vdocs='s documents.

*** measured on his live daemon after the migration

: [DATASET:install] office/vdocs  27 literals · 50 symbols · 27 linked
: [DATASET:install] office/corpo  33 literals · 33 symbols · 33 linked

and in the db: 27 literals owned by vdocs, 33 by corpo, 0 unowned; 26 scheme symbols shared, 51 coordinates
owned, 0 unowned.

** a path is not a url — the second one this session

=paladin.skills.resolve= (=subsystems/paladin/skills/fs.js:10=) built =new URL(path, "file://…").pathname=
and returned it as a FILESYSTEM path. =URL.pathname= escapes, so every path with a space came back
percent-encoded: =vdex_file= with a spaced title wrote =Notes%20on%20invoices.md= to disk, and =home()='s
=Deno.stat= then missed the directory it had just been given. Fixed the same way as the cargo route — the URL
is only the normaliser, the escapes come back off. Paladin 41 passed · 180 steps.

This is the same defect as =cargo-drops-any-path-with-a-space= in =known-issues=, in a second place. Both are
a path being handed to a URL and taken back unchanged. Worth a sweep for a third.

** what is red, and whose

| suite    | result                | the red                                                              |
|----------+-----------------------+----------------------------------------------------------------------|
| paladin  | 41 passed · 180 steps | —                                                                    |
| runtime  | 53 passed · 334 steps | =multiplayer= instance fixture no longer in =commons/instances/=      |

** SOLVED — the client listed two because integration pruned the two mountings every boot

His shot was /"i dont have the vdocs or corpo."/ — kajuit's daemon panel showed =vcompany 2=, =email= and
=calendar=. The daemon was right, the mounting was right, the db was NOT: it held five mode rows and neither
new one. The client reads mode ROWS (=dossier.js:118= =entities.mode.find({})=) and filters them on
=implements("application") || implements("conversational")= (=d.svelte:214=), so five rows minus three
traitless ones is exactly the two he saw.

The rows were created and then deleted, every boot, by =systems/runtime/daemon/lifecycle/integration.js=:

: const typeSlug = (item) => `${item.type}:${item.slug}`;
: const installed = new Set(modes.map((m) => typeSlug(m.manifest)));   // ← the MODULE
: await removeOrphans(em, await mode.find(), installed, typeSlug, "mode");

The keep-set is built from the module's MANIFEST (=office:vdex=); =population.modes()= ensures the row on the
MOUNTING slug (=office:vdocs=, =office:corpo=). Neither is in the set, so the uninstall sweep took both. The
boot log says it plainly and had been saying it all along:

: [VDEX] office/vdocs …/documentation/content 27 documents · 34 coordinates · pruned 0/0
: [VDEX] office/corpo /Users/finn/vivalence/coorporation 33 documents · 17 coordinates · pruned 0/0
: pruned mode: vdocs
: pruned mode: corpo

Same defect as =population.js= dropping =mask.slug=, second site: the module's slug used where the mounting's
belongs. FIX, landed on his go — a =Mode= carries =type= and =slug= itself, and its =slug= is already the
mounting's:

: -    const installed = new Set(modes.map((m) => typeSlug(m.manifest)));
: +    const installed = new Set(modes.map(typeSlug));

*** the leak it was feeding

=Literal.mode_id= is =on delete set null=, so every prune orphaned the rows the mounting had just installed;
the next boot's upsert keys =(slug, mode)= against a fresh mode id, missed the orphans, and INSERTED again.
MEASURED before the fix: 660 vdex literals for 60 distinct slugs — eleven copies of every document — and 605
symbols for 83. Compounding, one full set per boot. =UNIQUE (slug, mode_id)= does not stop it: SQLite treats
NULLs as distinct, so an unowned row has no unique protection at all.

*** measured after the fix, live

: mode rows          7 — domain/voffice · dummy/mail · dummy/calendar · office/email · office/calendar · office/vdocs · office/corpo
: literal ownership  office/vdocs 27 · office/corpo 33
: symbol ownership   office/vdocs 34 · office/corpo 17
: aperture           /daemon/vcompany/mode/office/vdocs 31 routes · …/office/corpo 31 routes
: client filter      4 rows carry APPLICATION — email · calendar · vdocs · corpo

No =pruned mode:= line. Runtime suite at baseline (53 passed · 334 steps; the one red is the =multiplayer=
instance fixture, not this). The leftovers wait on his wipe:

: delete from Literal where mode_id is null and ontology = 'vdex';                                    -- 180
: delete from Symbol  where mode_id is null and (slug like 'vdex.area.%'
:        or slug like 'vdex.category.%' or slug like 'vdex.id.%');                                    -- 165

which keeps the 60 + 51 freshly owned rows and the 18 shared scheme symbols.

*** the slug wiring, swept end to end (his order)

| container | site                              | reads                        |         |
|-----------+-----------------------------------+------------------------------+---------|
| paladin   | =vip.accioOne=                    | =mask.slug= preserved        | correct |
| paladin   | =pensieve.register=               | =manifest.slug= = the module | correct |
| runtime   | =Mode= ctor                       | =manifest.slug=              | module  |
| runtime   | =population.modes=                | =mask.slug ?? mode.slug=     | correct |
| runtime   | =integration.prune=               | =m.manifest=                 | WAS THE BUG |
| runtime   | =metadata /modes=                 | =mode.type= · =mode.slug=    | correct |
| runtime   | =aperture/modes findOne=          | =modes[where.slug]=          | correct |
| runtime   | =resolution:45 /metadata/manifest= | the module manifest          | ambiguous, unread |
| client    | =dossier.mount=                   | the mode ROW's slug          | correct |
| client    | =ModeDossier=                     | =ctx.entity.slug=            | correct |
| client    | =d.svelte:214=                    | trait filter, no slug        | correct |

One ambiguity left standing and NOT touched: =/mode/office/vdocs/metadata/manifest= answers =slug: "vdex"=,
the module's own name under a mounting's route. Nothing reads it for identity — the client takes identity from
the row — so it is a wording question, his call.


** manifest is the ONE identity record — declared, folded in paladin, consumed by the runtime (LANDED on /"go all"/)

His design, in his words: /"what if we standardize on manifest. no mode.slug at all. neither in the daemon.js
instance config … mode.module.manifest from registry, mode.manifest patched by instance. no mode.slug mode.type
etc outside manifest."/

It is not a new convention — it is the existing one applied one level down. EVERY daemon declaration already
spells identity as =manifest: {type, slug, version}= (brazilian · italian · spanish · vcompany). The kernel
entry was the only place in the tree spelling it flat, in exactly one file.

*** T1 — the declaration, folded where its sibling already lives

=vip.accioOne= already folded =statics= (=mask.statics = {…module.statics, …entry.statics}=), so
=mode.statics = mask.statics ?? mode.statics= was never a replace — the merge happened a layer up. The
manifest was the one field that never got the same treatment, which is the whole argument for paladin owning it.

: // subsystems/paladin/prototypes/vip.js — accioOne
: manifest: new Manifest({ ...service.manifest, ...(query.manifest ?? {}) }),
: statics: { ...(service.statics ?? {}), ...(query.statics ?? {}) },

=service.manifest= is never touched, so =mode.module.manifest= stays the REGISTRY identity while =mode.manifest=
is the MOUNTING's. =new Manifest= rather than a plain object keeps the =identifier= getter. The runtime stops
resolving declarations and only consumes:

: // systems/runtime/daemon/lifecycle/population.js — modes()
: mode.manifest = mask.manifest ?? mode.manifest;
: mode.statics  = mask.statics  ?? mode.statics;
: ...
: .ensure({ ...mode.manifest });

=mask.slug= is GONE. The kernel entry now reads =manifest: { slug: "vdocs" }=. And =name=, =description=,
=version= became declarable for free — the =VDex / VDex= complaint in his screenshot needs no name-fallback rule
invented, only a word in the declaration.

CHECKED before writing: =accioOne='s pass-through branch is =if (is.object(query) && query.manifest &&
!query.module) return query;= — guarded on =!query.module=, so an entry carrying BOTH falls through to the
resolver. No collision.

*** T2 — one owner on the citizen

=subsystems/typology/prototypes/mode.js= (typology, his blessing): =type= · =slug= · =traits= stopped being
data properties copied out of the manifest and became accessors ONTO it, so the two can no longer disagree —
which is the bug class that ate this whole session, twice (=population= dropping =mask.slug=, then
=integration.prune= reading =m.manifest=).

: Object.defineProperties(this, {
:   type:   { enumerable: true, get: () => this.manifest.type },
:   slug:   { enumerable: true, get: () => this.manifest.slug },
:   traits: { enumerable: true, get: () => this.manifest.traits ?? [] },
: });

MEASURED, and the reason for =defineProperties= over class getters: a class getter sits on the PROTOTYPE and is
NOT enumerable, so =Object.keys=, spread and every pojo fold silently drop identity. The first cut did exactly
that and broke =mode-instance.snapshot= — =pojo.type= came back =undefined=. Own ENUMERABLE accessors restore
serialization while keeping the single owner: spread invokes the getter and copies the value. Four test sites
spread a live mode (=dataset.install= ×3, =datasink.drain=) and would have lost its slug.

Second measurement worth keeping: that snapshot asserts =pojo.traits= is undefined even though =traits= IS
enumerable. Not a rule — =specimen='s =fold= carries a =WeakSet=, and =mode.traits= is the SAME array object as
=manifest.traits=, so the second visit is a cycle leaf. Identity through one owner is what makes that hold.

*** T3 — declined, and why

/"enforce throughout"/ read literally = delete the accessors, rewrite all 75 read sites (=mode.slug= 41 ·
=mode.type= 34; runtime 27 · kajuit 12 · instance 5 · typology 1 · fixtures 1). It cannot land on the client
anyway: kajuit's =Mode= is an ORM ROW with flat =slug=/=type= COLUMNS — the unique =(slug, type)=, every
=findOne({slug})= and the FK from literal all key on them. T3 buys nothing T2 does not, and pays a flag-day.

THE BOUNDARY, written down: **the citizen carries a manifest; the row stays flat.** The daemon-side =Mode= has
one identity record; the db row is a projection of it and keeps its columns.

*** measured after, live

: mode rows   7 — domain/voffice · office/vdocs · office/corpo · dummy/mail · dummy/calendar · office/email · office/calendar
: aperture    /daemon/vcompany/mode/office/vdocs 31 routes · …/office/corpo 31 routes
: ownership   office/vdocs 27 literals · office/corpo 33 · 23 unowned (the 16 event + 7 message scheme rows)

Suites: paladin 41 passed · 180 steps clean · typology 123 passed · 495 steps back AT baseline (the one red is
=hallucination snapshot · tool loop=, untouched by this) · runtime 53 passed · 334 steps, the one red the
=multiplayer= fixture.

OWED beef: a =name= in each entry if he wants the client to stop printing =VDex= twice —
=manifest: { slug: "vdocs", name: "…" }=, one word, now that it flows.

*** T3 — the full propagation (LANDED on /"go all"/, after the count was corrected)

His call: /"manifest also on mode prototype. no getters. on modeentity is the one exception that makes sense to
me."/ My earlier 75-site objection did not survive measurement — it was a raw grep over BOTH containers
including comments and exactly the row sites he was carving out. The real citizen surface was ~38 lines.

=Mode= is a plain data citizen again — =manifest= · =module= · =statics= · =secrets= · =mountpoint= · =mount= ·
=url= · =aperture= · =tools= · =entity= · =id=, with =implements()= the only method, over
=this.manifest.traits=. Every citizen read is =mode.manifest.*=.

**The type checker he asked for.** JS gives none, so a missed site would be =undefined= rather than an error.
The gate is =v.primitives.Manifest= at the pinhole where the mounting manifest is minted:

: // subsystems/paladin/prototypes/vip.js — accioOne
: const manifest = v.primitives.Manifest.cast({ ...service.manifest, ...(query.manifest ?? {}) });
: const faults = v.primitives.Manifest.faults(manifest);
: if (faults.length) throw new Error(`[VIP] ${query.module}: the kernel entry's manifest ${…}`);

MEASURED against the live schematic:

| input                       | cast                                          | faults                                              |
|-----------------------------+-----------------------------------------------+-----------------------------------------------------|
| module only, no declaration | ={type,slug:"vdex",traits:[]}=                 | none                                                |
| declaration renames it      | ={type,slug:"vdocs",traits:[]}=                | none                                                |
| slug dropped                | ={type,traits:[]}=                             | =/= must have required properties slug              |
| slug not a slug             | ={type,slug:"Not A Slug",traits:[]}=           | =/slug= must be lowercase alphanumeric words joined by single dashes |

Two deltas made that work. =schematics/primitives/manifest.js= gives =traits= =.optional().default([])= — the
default is what makes ~50 direct =mode.manifest.traits= reads safe, and =.optional()= is required beside it
because a typebox default does NOT imply optional (the first cut made =traits= REQUIRED and broke =v.test.js=).
And =scalars/index.js='s =slug= gained a =title=, because =v.js='s faults nicety prints =held.title= in place of
a regex and the slug scalar had none — a bad kernel slug used to report =must match pattern "^[a-z0-9]+…"=.

**The lesson, and it cost three red rounds.** Deleting a property that ALIASES another is not a
=grep "mode\.slug"= job — the misses are aliases. Four sites read the same citizen under another name and none
matched the pattern:

| site                                        | alias     |
|---------------------------------------------+-----------|
| =daemon/traits/agentic.js=                  | =source=  |
| =modes/office/email/provider.js=            | =known=   |
| =modes/office/calendar/provider.js=         | =known=   |
| =daemon/lifecycle/integration.js=           | =mode= applied to a ROW |

The sweep that finds them is over the CONSUMERS, not the spelling: =grep "flatmodes()\|\.modes\["= enumerates
every place a live mode is handed out, and each provider's =find((known) => known.type === type)= falls out of
it immediately. Second trap: seven hand-built mode stubs in the suites are plain objects, so each needed a
=manifest= (=dataset.install= · =datasink.drain= ×2 · =datasink.roundtrip= ×2 · =traits.test= BOOTED ×2 ·
=voice.conversation=). Third: I renamed two ROW sites by mistake and had to revert both —
=aperture.snapshot.test.js= reads =/metadata/modes= (the wire) and =corpus.snapshot.test.js= reads
=entities.mode.findAll()=. Classify before renaming.

**The best single argument for the whole change**, found while fixing it: =integration.prune='s keep-set is
=typeSlug(mode.manifest)= — character for character the expression that was THE BUG earlier this session. It
was wrong when =manifest= meant the module and it is right now that it means the mounting. Nothing about the
line changed; the vocabulary underneath it did.

**What stayed flat, by his ruling.** =ModeEntity= (unique =(slug, type)=, the FK from literal/symbol), the
=/metadata/modes= wire projection, kajuit's client =Mode=, and =commons/fixtures/data/live.js= which reads that
wire. Gate after the landing — every surviving =mode.slug=/=type=/=traits= in the tree is one of those, plus
five dead comments.

Suites: paladin 41 · 180 clean · kajuit 35 · 119 clean · typology 123 · 495 and runtime 53 · 334, both AT
baseline. Live: 7 mode rows, =office/vdocs= 31 routes · =office/corpo= 31, 27 + 33 literals owned.


** the email thread walker took every child for a message (LANDED — modes/** is mine)

=~/.viva/registry/vcompany/modes/office/email/aperture/index.js=, =thread()=. =one()= filters
=ontology: "message"=, but =uses=/=in= is ONE graph shared by every ontology and dummy/calendar links its events
to the mail they came from (the =link:literal.uses 7/7= in the boot log). So =c77d10a9.in= = ={b310ff47,
ev04, ev13}=, =one(ctx, "ev04")= → null, =seen.set(full.slug, full)= threw. Five messages carry event children
directly and the other two walk up into one that does — ALL SEVEN threads 500'd, which is what his console
showed. Both hops now filter on =known.ontology === "message"=: a mail thread is messages; the events that
reference one are the calendar's view of it, not replies.

=office/calendar= never had it — =/event= resolves each =uses= ref BY ID (=aperture/index.js:40=), which is the
shape email should have had. One landmine left there, not a fault: =/mint= does =findOne({ slug: input.from })=
with no ontology and no mode, and two mounts may now legally hold one slug (measured: 0 collisions today).

Suites: email 2 passed · 5 steps · calendar + both dummies 16 passed · 58 steps.


** RULING (his, twice, and I still wrote it wrong once) — the ontology is not the module

/"the vdex as ontology is CORRECT! ontology is the same. i just want a second mountpoint."/ then
/"no. youre still considereing ontology level. this is purely mode slug."/ then, on my sentence calling the
=vdex= symbol a description of the module: /"THE FUCKING VDEX ONTOLOGY MARKER DOESNT DESCRIBE THE FUCKING
MODULE"/.

The =vdex= symbol marks the ONTOLOGY. One ontology, however many mounts carry it, whatever module mounts them.
A literal's =ontology= field is its KIND and is right to be identical across both mountings; the thing that
distinguishes two mountings is the MODE, and nothing else. Everything scoped in this landing is scoped by mode.
Nothing is scoped by ontology, and nothing should be.
